theme_from_system builds the theme from the desktop's own colour scheme: a Windows contrast theme or a KDE colour scheme (`Ev_Theme.palette`). In high contrast the colours are used exactly — the person chose them to read by — on the structure of the toolkit's contrast theme; otherwise the scheme goes through theme_from_palette, which keeps text readable. `accent`, the app's own, takes the selection colour's place except in high contrast.

### `theme_on_ground`

```odin
theme_on_ground :: proc(base: Theme, bg, ink: Color, accent: Color = {}, name := "") -> (th: Theme)
```

theme_on_ground derives a whole theme for a ground an app picks: a reader's Paper, Sepia, Dusk or Night page, a photo editor's dark canvas, a terminal's own colours. `bg` is the ground, `ink` the text on it, `accent` what a link or a primary button is made of (the base's own accent when it is left out).  What comes back is `base` with **only its colours re-derived**: the type, the radii, the spacing, the motion and the desktop's accessibility preferences are the ones the app is already running with, so a page in the app's colours still obeys reduce motion, reduce transparency, high contrast and the desktop's text size. The ladder of surfaces steps away from the ground toward whichever side has room — lighter over a dark page, darker over cream paper, where stepping lighter would run out of white — and every pair that carries text is put through `ensure_contrast`, so a ghost button on Sepia hovers in a darker sepia and still reads, rather than in the desktop's grey.  Give it to a part of a window with `theme_area`.

## theme_style.odin

Component styles: one struct per widget kind on the theme (`th.button`, `th.field`, …). Every field is a Maybe: nil derives from the global tokens exactly as the toolkit always has, so a theme sets only what it changes, and 0 or a transparent colour are values of their own. Widgets read a resolved look (`button_look(th)`, …), never the style itself, so defaults and density live here.

`Theme.density` moves control heights by DENSITY_STEP a unit (compact −1, spacious +1), and where padding makes a height, each side by half that.

### `DENSITY_STEP`

```odin
DENSITY_STEP :: f32(4)
```

DENSITY_STEP is how far one unit of Theme.density moves a control's height, logical px.

### `Text_Override`

```odin
Text_Override :: struct {
	size:   Maybe(f32),
	weight: Maybe(Font_Weight),
	family: Maybe(string), // named as Theme.font_family is; the app's string
}
```

Text_Override restyles a text a widget sets itself; nil keeps the toolkit's. A size is logical px at text scale 1 and follows the text scale, as a role's does.

### `Text_Look`

```odin
Text_Look :: struct {
	role:   Text_Role,
	size:   f32,
	weight: Font_Weight,
	family: string,
}
```

Text_Look is a resolved Text_Override. A size of 0 is the role's own; a family of "" is the role's, or the theme's.

### `Button_Colors`

```odin
Button_Colors :: struct {
	fill:         Maybe(Color),        // the background
	fill_bottom:  Maybe(Color),        // the gradient's bottom stop; {} is flat
	ink:          Maybe(Color),        // label and icons, and a mark that names no colour
	border:       Maybe(Color),
	border_width: Maybe(f32),          // drawn inside the edge, taking no room
	shadow:       Maybe(Shadow_Style),
}
```

Button_Colors is one look of a button. Hover and press washes still derive from `fill`, and a disabled button still dims by state.disabled_alpha.

### `Button_Size_Style`

```odin
Button_Size_Style :: struct {
	padding:        Maybe([4]f32), // a labelled button's: top, end, bottom, start
	icon_only_size: Maybe(f32),    // an icon-only button's side: it is square
	text:           Text_Override, // the label
}
```

Button_Size_Style is one Button_Size's metrics.

### `Button_Style`

```odin
Button_Style :: struct {
	radius:       Maybe(f32), // every variant and size
	gap:          Maybe(f32), // between the icons, the picture and the label
	icon_size:    Maybe(f32), // an icon or theme icon; button_options' icon_size wins
	image_size:   Maybe(f32), // a picture (an avatar); button_options' icon_size wins
	image_radius: Maybe(f32),
	regular:      Button_Size_Style,
	small:        Button_Size_Style,
	primary, secondary, outline, ghost, danger: Button_Colors,
	pressed:      Button_Colors, // a toggle that is on: Secondary, Outline, Ghost (Primary and Danger keep their fill)
	unpressed:    Button_Colors, // a toggle that is off: Primary, Secondary, Danger (Outline and Ghost keep their look)
	mark_thickness:     Maybe(f32),   // a Button_Mark that names none
	progress_thickness: Maybe(f32),   // the bar; never more than a third of the icon
	progress_fill:      Maybe(Color), // the value; on a fill it cannot be seen on, the ink that reads there instead
	progress_track:     Maybe(Color), // the rest of the bar or ring
	ring_thickness:     Maybe(f32),
	ring_gap:           Maybe(f32),   // between the icon's box and the ring
	badge_fill:         Maybe(Color), // a Button_Badge with no colour of its own
	badge_ink:          Maybe(Color), // its text
	badge_dot:          Maybe(f32),   // a dot's diameter
	badge_height:       Maybe(f32),   // a pill's height
	badge_padding:      Maybe(f32),   // each side of a pill's text
	badge_text:         Text_Override,
}
```

Button_Style restyles every `button`, the toolkit's own included. Nil derives from the global tokens; density then moves the heights.

### `Button_Colors_Look`

```odin
Button_Colors_Look :: struct {
	fill, fill_bottom, ink, border: Color,
	border_width:                   f32,
	shadow:                         Shadow_Style,
}
```

Button_Colors_Look is a resolved Button_Colors.

### `Button_Size_Look`

```odin
Button_Size_Look :: struct {
	padding:           [4]f32,
	icon_only_padding: [4]f32,
	text:              Text_Look, // size 0: the Label step's
}
```

Button_Size_Look is a resolved Button_Size_Style, density applied.

### `Button_Look`

```odin
Button_Look :: struct {
	radius, gap, icon_size, image_size, image_radius: f32,
	sizes:              [Button_Size]Button_Size_Look,
	colors:             [Button_Variant]Button_Colors_Look,
	pressed, unpressed: Button_Colors_Look,
	mark_thickness, progress_thickness, ring_thickness, ring_gap: f32,
	progress_fill, progress_track: Color,
	badge_fill, badge_ink:         Color,
	badge_dot, badge_height, badge_padding: f32,
	badge_text:                    Text_Look,
}
```

Button_Look is everything a button draws with. Widgets read this, never Theme.button.

### `button_look`

```odin
button_look :: proc(th: ^Theme) -> (lk: Button_Look)
```

button_look resolves Theme.button. Call it with the theme in force where the button is built or painted (a popup's, glass's), never a cached one.

### `Icon_Toggle_Style`

```odin
Icon_Toggle_Style :: struct {
	size:         Maybe(f32),   // the diameter; the builder's `size` wins
	icon_size:    Maybe(f32),   // nil: half the diameter, rounded
	fill:         Maybe(Color), // off
	ink:          Maybe(Color), // the icon, off
	pressed_fill: Maybe(Color), // on
	pressed_ink:  Maybe(Color), // the icon, on
}
```

Icon_Toggle_Style restyles every `icon_toggle`. Hover and press washes still derive from the fills.

### `Icon_Toggle_Look`

```odin
Icon_Toggle_Look :: struct {
	size:      f32,
	icon_size: f32, // 0: half the drawn diameter, rounded (the diameter is per instance)
	fill, ink, pressed_fill, pressed_ink: Color,
}
```

Icon_Toggle_Look is a resolved Icon_Toggle_Style, density applied.

### `icon_toggle_look`

```odin
icon_toggle_look :: proc(th: ^Theme) -> (lk: Icon_Toggle_Look)
```

### `Field_Style`

```odin
Field_Style :: struct {
	padding:            Maybe([4]f32), // a text field's: text_input, number_input, search_field's text, the hex field
	dropdown_padding:   Maybe([4]f32), // a closed drop-down's: select, date_field, time_field, color_field
	min_height:         Maybe(f32),    // nil: none; the height is a line of text plus padding
	inset:              Maybe(f32),    // edge to a leading magnifier or swatch, and to search_field's clear cross
	gap:                Maybe(f32),    // a drop-down's value to its icon
	radius:             Maybe(f32),
	border_width:       Maybe(f32),
	focus_border_width: Maybe(f32),    // nil: border_width
	fill:               Maybe(Color),
	border:             Maybe(Color),
	focus_border:       Maybe(Color),  // a focused field, an open drop-down
	ink:                Maybe(Color),  // typed text and a drop-down's value
	placeholder:        Maybe(Color),  // a placeholder, and a drop-down with nothing picked
	icon:               Maybe(Color),  // a drop-down's icon or chevron, the magnifier, the clear cross
	icon_size:          Maybe(f32),    // a drop-down's icon, the stepper glyphs
	caret:              Maybe(Color),  // nil: ink
	caret_width:        Maybe(f32),
	text:               Text_Override, // nil parts: the body step
	compact:            Field_Compact_Style,
	stepper:            Field_Stepper_Style,
	search:             Field_Search_Style,
	swatch:             Field_Swatch_Style,
	inline_card:        Field_Card_Style,
}
```

Field_Style is the look the single-line fields share: text_input, number_input, search_field, and the closed select, date_field, time_field and color_field (and time_picker's frame). Nil derives from the global tokens; hover and press still mix in from the state layers.

### `Field_Compact_Style`

```odin
Field_Compact_Style :: struct {
	padding: Maybe([4]f32),
	radius:  Maybe(f32),
}
```

Field_Compact_Style is an editor standing in for a label: a table cell's or a tree item's (text_input's `compact`).

### `Field_Stepper_Style`

```odin
Field_Stepper_Style :: struct {
	size:   Maybe(f32),   // square
	gap:    Maybe(f32),   // field to buttons, and between them
	radius: Maybe(f32),   // nil: the field's
	fill:   Maybe(Color), // nil: the field's
	ink:    Maybe(Color), // the glyphs; nil: the field's ink
}
```

Field_Stepper_Style is number_input's − and + buttons.

### `Field_Search_Style`

```odin
Field_Search_Style :: struct {
	gap:             Maybe(f32), // magnifier, text and clear cross
	icon_size:       Maybe(f32), // the magnifier
	clear_size:      Maybe(f32), // the clear cross's round target
	clear_text_size: Maybe(f32), // the × in it, before the desktop's text scale: text() applies that
}
```

Field_Search_Style is what search_field adds to a field.

### `Field_Swatch_Style`

```odin
Field_Swatch_Style :: struct {
	size:   Maybe(f32),
	radius: Maybe(f32),
}
```

Field_Swatch_Style is color_field's swatch.

### `Field_Card_Style`

```odin
Field_Card_Style :: struct {
	padding:   Maybe([4]f32),
	radius:    Maybe(f32),          // nil: compact's
	fill:      Maybe(Color),
	border:    Maybe(Color),        // nil: focus_border, as it always has the keyboard
	shadow:    Maybe(Shadow_Style),
	text_size: Maybe(f32),
}
```

Field_Card_Style is the wrapping editor laid over an icon view's label to rename it. Opaque, since it covers the tiles around it.

### `Field_Look`

```odin
Field_Look :: struct {
	padding:            [4]f32,
	dropdown_padding:   [4]f32,
	min_height:         f32, // 0: none
	inset:              f32,
	gap:                f32,
	radius:             f32,
	border_width:       f32,
	focus_border_width: f32,
	fill:               Color,
	border:             Color,
	focus_border:       Color,
	ink:                Color,
	placeholder:        Color,
	icon:               Color,
	icon_size:          f32,
	caret:              Color,
	caret_width:        f32,
	text_size:          f32,         // final px: the text scale is already in it
	text_weight:        Font_Weight, // typed text
	family_name:        string,    // a family the style names; "" uses text_family / value_family
	text_family:        Family_Id,
	value_weight:       Font_Weight, // a drop-down's value, set as a body text is
	value_family:       Family_Id,
	compact:            Field_Compact_Look,
	stepper:            Field_Stepper_Look,
	search:             Field_Search_Look,
	swatch:             Field_Swatch_Look,
	inline_card:        Field_Card_Look,
}
```

Field_Look is Field_Style resolved, density applied. Widgets read this, never the style.

### `Field_Compact_Look`

```odin
Field_Compact_Look :: struct {
	padding: [4]f32,
	radius:  f32,
}
```

### `Field_Stepper_Look`

```odin
Field_Stepper_Look :: struct {
	size, gap, radius: f32,
	fill, ink:         Color,
}
```

### `Field_Search_Look`

```odin
Field_Search_Look :: struct {
	gap, icon_size, clear_size: f32,
	clear_text_size:            f32, // unscaled: passed through text(size =)
}
```

### `Field_Swatch_Look`

```odin
Field_Swatch_Look :: struct {
	size, radius: f32,
}
```

### `Field_Card_Look`

```odin
Field_Card_Look :: struct {
	padding:              [4]f32,
	radius, border_width: f32,
	fill, border:         Color,
	shadow:               Shadow_Style,
	text_size:            f32,
}
```

### `field_look`

```odin
field_look :: proc(th: ^Theme) -> Field_Look
```

field_look resolves the theme's Field_Style: nil from the global tokens, then density on the heights.

### `Check_Style`

```odin
Check_Style :: struct {
	indicator_size:       Maybe(f32),    // the checkbox's box and the radio's ring, across
	gap:                  Maybe(f32),    // indicator to label
	radius:               Maybe(f32),    // a row's corners: its hover wash and focus ring
	text:                 Text_Override, // the label
	ink:                  Maybe(Color),  // the label
	border:               Maybe(Color),  // an unchecked indicator's edge
	selected_fill:        Maybe(Color),  // a checked box; a picked radio's ring and dot
	padding:              Maybe([4]f32), // the checkbox's row
	indicator_radius:     Maybe(f32),    // the checkbox's box corners
	border_width:         Maybe(f32),    // the checkbox's box edge
	fill:                 Maybe(Color),  // an unchecked box
	selected_ink:         Maybe(Color),  // the tick
	radio_padding:        Maybe([4]f32), // one radio option's row
	radio_border_width:   Maybe(f32),    // the radio's ring
	radio_gap:            Maybe(f32),    // between options down the page
	radio_gap_horizontal: Maybe(f32),    // between options across it (`horizontal`)
}
```

Check_Style is the checkbox and the radio. Their indicator, row and colours are shared so the two match; a field named for one applies to it alone. A radio's ring and dot are round on purpose and take no radius.

### `Check_Look`

```odin
Check_Look :: struct {
	indicator_size, gap, radius:     f32,
	text:                            Text_Look,
	ink, border, selected_fill:      Color,
	padding:                         [4]f32,
	indicator_radius, border_width:  f32,
	fill, selected_ink:              Color,
	radio_padding:                   [4]f32,
	radio_border_width:              f32,
	radio_gap, radio_gap_horizontal: f32,
}
```

### `check_look`

```odin
check_look :: proc(th: ^Theme) -> Check_Look
```

### `Toggle_Style`

```odin
Toggle_Style :: struct {
	track_width:    Maybe(f32),
	track_height:   Maybe(f32),
	thumb_size:     Maybe(f32),          // the thumb, across
	gap:            Maybe(f32),          // switch to label
	padding:        Maybe([4]f32),       // the row
	radius:         Maybe(f32),          // the row's corners: its hover wash and focus ring
	text:           Text_Override,       // the label
	ink:            Maybe(Color),        // the label
	track:          Maybe(Color),        // off
	selected_track: Maybe(Color),        // on
	border:         Maybe(Color),        // the off track's edge; it fades out as the switch comes on
	border_width:   Maybe(f32),
	thumb:          Maybe(Color),        // off
	selected_thumb: Maybe(Color),        // on
	thumb_shadow:   Maybe(Shadow_Style),
}
```

Toggle_Style is the switch. Its track and thumb stay round whatever the radii: a switch is read by its shape before its colour.

### `Toggle_Look`

```odin
Toggle_Look :: struct {
	track_width, track_height, thumb_size: f32,
	gap:                                   f32,
	padding:                               [4]f32,
	radius:                                f32,
	text:                                  Text_Look,
	ink, track, selected_track, border:    Color,
	border_width:                          f32,
	thumb, selected_thumb:                 Color,
	thumb_shadow:                          Shadow_Style,
}
```

### `toggle_look`

```odin
toggle_look :: proc(th: ^Theme) -> Toggle_Look
```

### `Slider_Style`

```odin
Slider_Style :: struct {
	width:   Maybe(f32),           // when the call gives none
	regular: Slider_Regular_Style,
	large:   Slider_Large_Style,
	readout: Slider_Readout_Style, // the value shown above it (slider_options' `format`)
}
```

Slider_Style is the slider at both sizes. Its thumbs stay round whatever the radii, and a Large slider stays a pill: its knob is as tall as its track.

### `Slider_Regular_Style`

```odin
Slider_Regular_Style :: struct {
	height:             Maybe(f32),          // the slider's own; track and thumb are centred in it
	track_height:       Maybe(f32),
	thumb_size:         Maybe(f32),          // across
	thumb_border_width: Maybe(f32),
	track:              Maybe(Color),        // the groove past the thumb
	fill:               Maybe(Color),        // the groove up to the thumb, and the thumb's hover halo
	thumb:              Maybe(Color),
	thumb_border:       Maybe(Color),        // over a plain track; a sampled one (a colour picker) keeps `surface`
	thumb_shadow:       Maybe(Shadow_Style),
}
```

### `Slider_Large_Style`

```odin
Slider_Large_Style :: struct {
	height:       Maybe(f32),          // the pill, and its knob
	icon_size:    Maybe(f32),          // nil: 0.54 of the height
	track:        Maybe(Color),
	fill:         Maybe(Color),        // up to and under the knob, and the knob's hover halo
	thumb:        Maybe(Color),
	thumb_shadow: Maybe(Shadow_Style),
	fill_ink:     Maybe(Color),        // the icon over the fill
	thumb_ink:    Maybe(Color),        // the icon under the knob
	track_ink:    Maybe(Color),        // the icon on the track past the fill
}
```

### `Slider_Readout_Style`

```odin
Slider_Readout_Style :: struct {
	fill:   Maybe(Color),
	ink:    Maybe(Color),
	border: Maybe(Color),
	radius: Maybe(f32),
	text:   Text_Override,
}
```

### `Slider_Regular_Look`

```odin
Slider_Regular_Look :: struct {
	height, track_height, thumb_size, thumb_border_width: f32,
	track, fill, thumb, thumb_border:                     Color,
	thumb_shadow:                                         Shadow_Style,
}
```

### `Slider_Large_Look`

```odin
Slider_Large_Look :: struct {
	height, icon_size:              f32,
	track, fill, thumb:             Color,
	thumb_shadow:                   Shadow_Style,
	fill_ink, thumb_ink, track_ink: Color,
}
```

### `Slider_Readout_Look`

```odin
Slider_Readout_Look :: struct {
	fill, ink, border: Color,
	radius:            f32,
	text:              Text_Look,
}
```

Slider_Readout_Look: drawn straight to the layer, so `text.size` is the px drawn (never 0) and `text.weight` is the readout's own, not the caption's.

### `Slider_Look`

```odin
Slider_Look :: struct {
	width:   f32,
	regular: Slider_Regular_Look,
	large:   Slider_Large_Look,
	readout: Slider_Readout_Look,
}
```

### `slider_look`

```odin
slider_look :: proc(th: ^Theme) -> Slider_Look
```

### `Segmented_Style`

```odin
Segmented_Style :: struct {
	track_padding:   Maybe([4]f32),       // the groove around the segments
	padding_x:       Maybe(f32),          // a segment's sides while there is room
	min_padding_x:   Maybe(f32),          // …and the least they give way to
	padding_y:       Maybe(f32),
	gap:             Maybe(f32),          // icon to label
	icon_size:       Maybe(f32),
	radius:          Maybe(f32),          // the groove's corners, at most half its height
	segment_radius:  Maybe(f32),          // a segment's: its hover wash, the picked pill, the focus ring
	text:            Text_Override,
	track:           Maybe(Color),        // the groove; nil steps away from `selected_fill` until the two tell apart
	ink:             Maybe(Color),        // an unpicked segment's label and icon
	selected_fill:   Maybe(Color),        // the picked segment
	selected_ink:    Maybe(Color),
	selected_shadow: Maybe(Shadow_Style),
}
```

Segmented_Style is the segmented control: a groove of segments with the picked one raised in it. A segment's side padding gives way, from `padding_x` down to `min_padding_x`, before its label is cut.

### `Segmented_Look`

```odin
Segmented_Look :: struct {
	track_padding:                       [4]f32,
	padding_x, min_padding_x, padding_y: f32,
	gap, icon_size:                      f32,
	radius, segment_radius:              f32,
	text:                                Text_Look,
	track, ink, selected_fill, selected_ink: Color,
	selected_shadow:                     Shadow_Style,
}
```

### `segmented_look`

```odin
segmented_look :: proc(th: ^Theme) -> Segmented_Look
```

### `List_Row_Style`

```odin
List_Row_Style :: struct {
	min_height:      Maybe(f32),             // density moves it
	plain:           List_Row_Variant_Style, // variant = .Plain
	tiled:           List_Row_Variant_Style, // .Tile, .Accent, .Avatar
	title_large:     Text_Override,          // size = .Large, either variant
	icon_size:       Maybe(f32),             // a plain row's icon, glyph and picture; the end check and chevron
	image_radius:    Maybe(f32),             // a plain row's picture with image_round = false
	ink:             Maybe(Color),           // the title and a plain row's icons
	detail_ink:      Maybe(Color),           // secondary text: the detail, value and hint while nothing is unread
	selected_fill:   Maybe(Color),
	indicator:       Maybe(Color),           // the bar at the start of a selected or unread row
	indicator_width: Maybe(f32),
	indicator_inset: Maybe(f32),             // from the row's top and bottom
	tile:            List_Row_Tile_Style,
	badge:           List_Row_Badge_Style,
	hint:            List_Row_Hint_Style,
}
```

List_Row_Style is a theme's list_row. What differs between a plain row and a tiled one (Tile, Accent, Avatar) is in the two variant sub-styles.

### `List_Row_Variant_Style`

```odin
List_Row_Variant_Style :: struct {
	gap:     Maybe(f32),
	padding: Maybe([4]f32), // density moves top and bottom
	radius:  Maybe(f32),
	title:   Text_Override,
	detail:  Text_Override,
}
```

List_Row_Variant_Style is what a plain and a tiled row lay out differently.

### `List_Row_Tile_Style`

```odin
List_Row_Tile_Style :: struct {
	size:          Maybe(f32),
	radius:        Maybe(f32),
	icon_size:     Maybe(f32),
	app_icon_size: Maybe(f32),   // a theme icon, which fills the tile
	glyph_size:    Maybe(f32),   // a text size: before the text scale
	fill:          Maybe(Color),
	ink:           Maybe(Color),
	border:        Maybe(Color), // an empty square tile's 1 px outline
	accent_fill:   Maybe(Color), // variant = .Accent
	accent_ink:    Maybe(Color),
}
```

List_Row_Tile_Style is a tiled row's leading tile; an Avatar is a circle as wide.

### `List_Row_Badge_Style`

```odin
List_Row_Badge_Style :: struct {
	size:      Maybe(f32), // its height, and its width at one digit
	radius:    Maybe(f32),

	fill:      Maybe(Color),

}
```

List_Row_Badge_Style is the unread count's pill: a circle at one digit.

### `List_Row_Hint_Style`

	keycap_text:      Text_Override,

}
```

List_Row_Hint_Style is a selected row's hint: a keycap, then what it does.

### `list_row_look`

```odin

### `Tree_Row_Style`

```odin
Tree_Row_Style :: struct {
	min_height:      Maybe(f32),    // density moves it
	padding:         Maybe([4]f32), // density moves top and bottom
	gap:             Maybe(f32),    // between chevron, icon and label
	radius:          Maybe(f32),    // the row, and the keyboard cursor's ring around it
	indent:          Maybe(f32),    // per level; tree_view's own `indent` wins
	chevron_size:    Maybe(f32),    // the expander's slot, kept on a leaf so labels line up
	icon_size:       Maybe(f32),
	text:            Text_Override,
	ink:             Maybe(Color),  // label and icon
	branch_icon_ink: Maybe(Color),  // a branch's icon while it is not selected
	chevron_ink:     Maybe(Color),
	guide:           Maybe(Color),  // the indent guides, one hairline per level
	selected_fill:   Maybe(Color),
	selected_ink:    Maybe(Color),
}
```

Tree_Row_Style is a theme's tree_item rows, their indent guides and the tree_view's indent per level.

### `Tree_Row_Look`

Tree_Row_Look :: struct {
	min_height:      f32, // before target_min, which the row applies
	padding:         [4]f32,
	gap:             f32,
	radius:          f32,
	indent:          f32,
	chevron_size:    f32,
	icon_size:       f32,
	text:            Text_Look,
	ink:             Color,
	branch_icon_ink: Color,
	chevron_ink:     Color,
	guide:           Color,
	selected_fill:   Color,
	selected_ink:    Color,
}
```

```odin
tree_row_look :: proc "contextless" (th: ^Theme) -> Tree_Row_Look
```

tree_row_look resolves the theme's tree_row: nil from the global tokens, density applied. Paint reads it too (chevron, guides, cursor ring).

### `Table_Style`

```odin
Table_Style :: struct {
	row_height:          Maybe(f32),    // body rows; density moves it
	header_height:       Maybe(f32),    // nil: as tall as a row
	fill:                Maybe(Color),  // behind the rows
	border:              Maybe(Color),
	border_width:        Maybe(f32),
	radius:              Maybe(f32),
	header_fill:         Maybe(Color),
	header_ink:          Maybe(Color),  // headings and the sort arrow
	header_text:         Text_Override, // nil fields: the Label step
	cell_padding:        Maybe([4]f32), // header and body cells alike, so headings line up with their cells
	cell_gap:            Maybe(f32),    // between a cell's icon and its text
	cell_text:           Text_Override, // `cell_text`; nil fields: the Body step
	ink:                 Maybe(Color),  // `cell_text`'s text and icon
	muted_ink:           Maybe(Color),  // …when `muted`
	icon_size:           Maybe(f32),    // `cell_text`'s icon
	divider:             Maybe(Color),  // the hairlines between headings
	divider_width:       Maybe(f32),
	divider_inset:       Maybe(f32),    // kept clear above and below a divider
	selected_fill:       Maybe(Color),
	selected_ink:        Maybe(Color),  // `cell_text` in a selected row, muted cells too; nil: as unselected
	row_radius:          Maybe(f32),    // a row's corners; its selection, hover and cursor follow them
	cursor_border:       Maybe(Color),  // the keyboard's row
	cursor_border_width: Maybe(f32),
	indicator:           Maybe(Color),  // the resize guide and a column move's drop marker
	marquee:             Maybe(Color),  // the rubber band: its edge, and its fill at 15 %
}
```

Table_Style is the theme's say over every `table`; nil follows the global tokens. An app's own `row_height` replaces the row and header heights for that table.

### `Table_Look`

```odin
Table_Look :: struct {
	row_height, header_height:  f32,
	fill, border:               Color,
	border_width, radius:       f32,
	header_fill, header_ink:    Color,
	header_text:                Text_Look,
	cell_padding:               [4]f32,
	cell_gap:                   f32,
	cell_text:                  Text_Look,
	ink, muted_ink:             Color,
	icon_size:                  f32,
	divider:                    Color,
	divider_width:              f32,
	divider_inset:              f32,
	selected_fill:              Color,
	selected_ink:               Color,
	selected_muted_ink:         Color,
	row_radius:                 f32,
	cursor_border:              Color,
	cursor_border_width:        f32,
	indicator, marquee:         Color,
}
```

Table_Look is a table's style resolved against the theme, density applied.

### `table_look`

```odin
table_look :: proc(th: ^Theme) -> Table_Look
```

table_look is how every table draws under `th`. An app that zooms its rows scales `row_height` from here, so the theme and density still apply.

### `Icon_View_Style`

```odin
Icon_View_Style :: struct {
	tile:                Maybe([2]f32),  // an `.Icons` tile; its icon grows with it
	picture_tile:        Maybe([2]f32),  // a `.Pictures` tile
	gap:                 Maybe(f32),     // between tiles, both ways
	padding:             Maybe([4]f32),  // round the grid
	tile_padding:        Maybe([4]f32),  // inside an `.Icons` tile
	tile_gap:            Maybe(f32),     // between its icon and label
	picture_padding:     Maybe([4]f32),  // inside a `.Pictures` tile; the picture's square fits within it
	picture_gap:         Maybe(f32),     // between its picture, title and subtitle
	radius:              Maybe(f32),     // a tile's corners; its selection, hover and cursor follow them
	thumbnail_radius:    Maybe(f32),     // an `.Icons` tile's picture
	picture_radius:      Maybe(f32),     // a `.Pictures` tile's picture, or the square standing in for it
	placeholder_fill:    Maybe(Color),   // that square, for a picture tile with no picture
	placeholder_ink:     Maybe(Color),   // …and the icon on it
	ink:                 Maybe(Color),   // labels, titles and icons
	subtitle_ink:        Maybe(Color),
	selected_fill:       Maybe(Color),
	selected_ink:        Maybe(Color),   // everything on a selected tile, its subtitle too
	cursor_border:       Maybe(Color),   // the keyboard's tile
	cursor_border_width: Maybe(f32),
	label_text:          Text_Override,  // an `.Icons` label; nil fields: the Label step
	title_text:          Text_Override,  // a `.Pictures` title; nil fields: Label at Medium weight
	subtitle_text:       Text_Override,  // nil fields: the Caption step
	marquee:             Maybe(Color),   // the rubber band: its edge, and its fill at 15 %
}

## document.odin

Document is the editable text model shared by the text area, the code editor and the rich text / markdown editors: a sequence of blocks of UTF-8 text, edited in place by the widget the way `Text` is by a text input, with an undo journal. Positions are (block, byte offset) on grapheme boundaries.

Rich text lives in the same blocks: every block has a kind (paragraph, heading, list item, quote, code, rule) with its level / depth / checked state, and `runs` — attribute runs (bold, italic, underline, strike, code, link) covering the text in order. A block with no runs is plain. Text edits keep the runs consistent (typing continues the style at the caret, deleting removes what it covers, splitting and joining blocks split and join the runs), and every change — text, attributes, block kinds — is one journal entry, so undo restores formatting too.

### `Document`

```odin
Document :: struct {
	blocks:    [dynamic]Block,
	undo:      [dynamic]Edit,
	redo:      [dynamic]Edit,
	version:   u64, // bumps on every change; a cheap "did it change" for views
	stamper:   u64, // the last per-block stamp handed out (block_stamp)
	id:        u64, // unique per document: tells a new one from one it replaced at the same address, which `version` cannot
	allocator: runtime.Allocator,
}
```

Document is the editable text model behind `text_area`, `code_editor` and the rich-text and markdown editors: a sequence of `Block`s of UTF-8 text with a grouped undo journal. Positions are `(block, byte offset)` on grapheme boundaries. Make one with `document_make` and free it with `document_destroy`; the app owns it and it outlives any one view. `version` bumps on every change, which is a cheap "did it change" for a view to compare against.

### `Block_Kind`

```odin
Block_Kind :: enum u8 { Paragraph, Heading, Bullet, Numbered, Task, Quote, Code, Rule, Image, Table, Table_Sep }
```

Table: one row of a table, its cells written "| a | b |" in the text (consecutive Table blocks form one table; level 1 = a header row). Table_Sep is a markdown "|---|---|" line as the live view sees it.

### `table_cells`

```odin
table_cells :: proc(row: string, allocator := context.temp_allocator) -> [][2]int
```

table_cells splits a row's text into its cells' byte ranges (the text between the pipes, outer pipes optional, cell text trimmed of spaces). The ranges are in temp memory by default: copy what you keep.

### `table_sep_line`

```odin
table_sep_line :: proc(line: string) -> bool
```

table_sep_line reports a "|---|:--:|" separator line.

### `Table_Align`

```odin
Table_Align :: enum u8 { Default, Left, Center, Right }
```

Table_Align is how a table's column is set. `Default` is what a table that says nothing gets, and lets the renderer decide — which is not the same as `Left`, because a column of numbers reads better right-aligned and a table that has not asked for anything should get that.

### `table_sep_align`

```odin
table_sep_align :: proc(line: string, allocator := context.temp_allocator) -> []Table_Align
```

table_sep_align reads the alignment a "|---|:--:|---:|" line declares, one entry per column: `:---` left, `---:` right, `:---:` centred, and a bare `---` Default. Empty when `line` is not a separator.  The caller's memory.

### `table_cells_numeric`

```odin
table_cells_numeric :: proc(text: string, cells: [][2]int) -> bool
```

table_cells_numeric says whether every non-empty cell of `cells` in `text` reads as a number: digits, with grouping and decimal marks, a currency symbol or a percent sign, and a leading minus. Empty cells are skipped, but a column with nothing in it is not numeric. It lets a table that declares no alignment still right-align its figures, and is strict on purpose: "P001" has letters, so a column of part numbers stays put.

### `Block`

```odin
Block :: struct {
	text:    [dynamic]u8,
	kind:    Block_Kind,
	level:   u8,   // heading: 1..6
	depth:   u8,   // list nesting: 0..
	checked: bool, // task item
	lang:    string, // code block language name (owned; "" = none)
	src:     string, // image source (a path or URL; owned); the text is the alt text
	// A table header row's declared column alignment, two bits a column and
	// up to 32 of them (table_align_get / table_align_set), so a markdown
	// `|---:|` survives a save. A plain number rather than a slice: it copies
	// with the block, so undo, redo and duplication keep it without owning
	// anything.
	align:   u64,
	runs:    [dynamic]Run, // attribute runs in order, covering [0, len(text)); empty = all plain
	// What this block's content is, as a number: `block_stamp` gives an
	// unstamped block the next one, and every change to its text, runs or
	// meta clears it, so the same stamp means the block is as it was.
	// 0 = unstamped. It lets a view keep per-block work across an edit,
	// where `doc.version` changes for the whole document on every keystroke.
	stamp:   u64,
}
```

Block is one paragraph, heading, list item or code block of a `Document`: its UTF-8 text, which kind it is, how deep it sits in a list, and the attribute runs over its bytes.

### `block_stamp`

```odin
block_stamp :: proc(doc: ^Document, i: int) -> u64
```

block_stamp names block `i`'s content. The same number on two reads means the block has not changed in between; a block that has been edited, or is new, takes the next number, and a number is never used twice in one document — so a cache keyed by it can also survive blocks being inserted and removed above.  if h, ok := cache[block_stamp(doc, b)]; ok do return h   // still true

### `table_align_get`

```odin
table_align_get :: proc "contextless" (packed: u64, i: int) -> Table_Align
```

table_align_get is column `i`'s alignment out of a header block's packed `align`. Columns past the 32 it holds read as Default.

### `table_align_set`

```odin
table_align_set :: proc "contextless" (packed: u64, i: int, a: Table_Align) -> u64
```

table_align_set puts column `i`'s alignment into a packed value.

### `table_align_pack`

```odin
table_align_pack :: proc(cols: []Table_Align) -> (packed: u64)
```

table_align_pack is a whole row's alignment as one value.

### `Block_Meta`

```odin
Block_Meta :: struct {
	kind:    Block_Kind,
	level:   u8,
	depth:   u8,
	checked: bool,
	lang:    string,
	src:     string, // image source
	align:   u64,    // a table header row's declared column alignment (see Block.align)
}
```

Block_Meta is everything about a block but its text and runs.

### `Attr`

```odin
Attr :: enum u8 { Bold, Italic, Underline, Strike, Code, Link, Highlight, Mark } // Highlight: text washed as with a highlighter pen (markdown `==text==`); Mark: a markdown mark shown dimmed (display only, never in a document)
```

Attr is one character attribute over a document's text — the formatting a rich or markdown editor applies. `Attrs` is a set of them, and `Run` carries one set over a stretch of bytes.

### `Attrs`

```odin
Attrs :: bit_set[Attr; u8]
```

### `Run`

```odin
Run :: struct {
	end:   int,
	attrs: Attrs,
	href:  string, // owned; only with .Link
}
```

Run is one attribute run: it covers the bytes from the previous run's `end` (or 0) up to `end`.

### `Pos`

```odin
Pos :: struct {
	block, off: int,
}
```

Pos is a caret position: block index and byte offset inside it.

### `Edit_Kind`

```odin
Edit_Kind :: enum u8 { Insert, Delete, Runs, Meta }
```

Edit_Kind is what one entry in a document's undo journal did.

### `Edit`

```odin
Edit :: struct {
	kind:       Edit_Kind,
	at:         Pos,
	text:       string,   // owned by the document's allocator
	group:      u64,
	runs:       []Run,    // Insert / Delete: the text's runs; Runs: the block's runs before
	runs_after: []Run,    // Runs: the block's runs after
	metas:      []Block_Meta, // Insert / Delete: the metas of the blocks after the first (one per newline)
	meta, meta_after: Block_Meta, // Meta: before / after
}
```

Edit is one journal entry. Insert / Delete carry the text and its runs (offsets relative to the text; the text may span blocks) and the metas of the blocks the text creates / removed; Runs and Meta carry the old and new state of one block. Entries sharing a `group` undo and redo together (a typed word, a paste, a toolbar action over many blocks).

### `document_make`

```odin
document_make :: proc(initial := "", allocator := context.allocator) -> Document
```

### `document_destroy`

```odin
document_destroy :: proc(doc: ^Document)
```

### `document_set_text`

```odin
document_set_text :: proc(doc: ^Document, s: string)
```

document_set_text replaces everything (no undo entry) with `s`, one plain paragraph per line.

### `document_text`

```odin
document_text :: proc(doc: ^Document, allocator := context.allocator) -> string
```

document_text joins the blocks with '\n'. The string is the caller's to free (it is allocated with `allocator`, the context's by default).

### `document_range_text`

```odin
document_range_text :: proc(doc: ^Document, a, b: Pos, allocator := context.allocator) -> string
```

document_range_text is the text between two positions, the caller's to free as document_text's is.

### `block_str`

```odin
block_str :: proc(doc: ^Document, i: int) -> string
```

block_str is block `i`'s text as it stands. It aliases the document's own storage: the next edit to that block invalidates it, so copy what you keep.

### `block_count`

```odin
block_count :: proc(doc: ^Document) -> int
```

### `block_meta`

```odin
block_meta :: proc(doc: ^Document, i: int) -> Block_Meta
```

block_meta is a block's kind and settings (the lang string is borrowed).

### `document_start`

```odin
document_start :: proc() -> Pos
```

### `document_end`

```odin
document_end :: proc(doc: ^Document) -> Pos
```

### `document_rune_count`

```odin
document_rune_count :: proc(doc: ^Document) -> (n: int)
```

document_rune_count counts characters (block breaks count as one each).

### `document_len`

```odin
document_len :: proc(doc: ^Document) -> (n: int)
```

### `pos_less`

```odin
pos_less :: proc "contextless" (a, b: Pos) -> bool
```

### `document_flat_off`

```odin
document_flat_off :: proc(doc: ^Document, p: Pos) -> int
```

document_flat_off is a position as a byte offset into `document_text` — the blocks joined with a newline apiece. The accessibility bridge counts this way, and so does a selection that runs across several documents.

### `document_pos_at`

```odin
document_pos_at :: proc(doc: ^Document, off: int) -> Pos
```

document_pos_at is the other way: the position a byte offset into `document_text` names. Past the end is the end.

### `pos_order`

```odin
pos_order :: proc "contextless" (a, b: Pos) -> (lo, hi: Pos)
```

### `pos_clamp`

```odin
pos_clamp :: proc(doc: ^Document, p: Pos) -> Pos
```

### `pos_next_grapheme`

```odin
pos_next_grapheme :: proc(doc: ^Document, p: Pos) -> Pos
```

### `pos_prev_grapheme`

```odin
pos_prev_grapheme :: proc(doc: ^Document, p: Pos) -> Pos
```

### `pos_next_word`

```odin
pos_next_word :: proc(doc: ^Document, p: Pos) -> Pos
```

### `pos_prev_word`

```odin
pos_prev_word :: proc(doc: ^Document, p: Pos) -> Pos
```

### `attrs_at`

```odin
attrs_at :: proc(doc: ^Document, p: Pos) -> Attrs
```

attrs_at is the style at `p`: of the character before it (what typing there continues), else of the one after; plain at an empty block.

### `href_at`

```odin
href_at :: proc(doc: ^Document, p: Pos) -> string
```

href_at is the link target at `p`, "" when none. It aliases the run's own storage: an edit to that block invalidates it.

### `document_replace`

```odin
document_replace :: proc(doc: ^Document, a, b: Pos, s: string, group: u64 = 0, style: Maybe(Run) = nil) -> Pos
```

document_replace deletes [a, b) and inserts `s` there (newlines split blocks; a new block takes the first one's kind, a plain paragraph after a heading or a rule). Typed text continues the style at `a` unless `style` says otherwise. Both steps share `group` in the journal. Returns the position after the insertion.

### `document_set_attrs`

```odin
document_set_attrs :: proc(doc: ^Document, a, b: Pos, add, remove: Attrs, href := "", group: u64 = 0)
```

document_set_attrs adds `add` and removes `remove` over [a, b) (a link's `href` with .Link in `add`), block by block, as one journal group; the other attributes of the text stay.

### `document_attrs`

```odin
document_attrs :: proc(doc: ^Document, a, b: Pos) -> (all, any: Attrs)
```

document_attrs reports the style over [a, b): the attributes every character has (`all`) and the ones any has (`any`); at a caret, the style typing would continue.

### `document_set_meta`

```odin
document_set_meta :: proc(doc: ^Document, block: int, meta: Block_Meta, group: u64 = 0)
```

document_set_meta changes a block's kind and settings, journaled.

### `document_undo`

```odin
document_undo :: proc(doc: ^Document) -> (caret: Pos, ok: bool)
```

document_undo reverts the latest group. Returns the caret position to restore and whether anything was undone.

### `document_redo`

```odin
document_redo :: proc(doc: ^Document) -> (caret: Pos, ok: bool)
```

### `numbered_index`

```odin
numbered_index :: proc(doc: ^Document, b: int) -> int
```

numbered_index is the number a numbered item shows: its place among the numbered items of its depth in the same list (deeper items of any list kind between them do not break the count).

### `table_cell_at`

```odin
table_cell_at :: proc(row: string, off: int) -> int
```

table_cell_at is the index of the cell holding byte `off` of a row.

### `table_cell_start`

```odin
table_cell_start :: proc(row: string, i: int) -> int
```

table_cell_start is where cell `i`'s text starts (a new cell's text goes there).

### `table_row_empty`

```odin
table_row_empty :: proc(row: string) -> bool
```

table_row_empty reports a row whose cells hold no text.

### `table_empty_row`

```odin
table_empty_row :: proc(n: int, allocator := context.temp_allocator) -> string
```

table_empty_row writes a row of `n` empty cells: "|  |  |", in temp memory by default.

## markdown.odin

Markdown in and out of the Document, and a tokenizer for markdown source in the code editor. The dialect is CommonMark's everyday subset: ATX headings, bullet (- / *) and numbered (1.) lists nested by indentation, task items (- [ ] / - [x]), block quotes (>), fenced code blocks (``` with a language), thematic breaks (---), and inline **bold**, *italic*, ~~strike~~, `code` and [links](url). Everything else stays text.

### `document_markdown`

```odin
document_markdown :: proc(doc: ^Document, allocator := context.allocator) -> string
```

document_markdown prints the document as markdown. The string is the caller's to free (allocated with `allocator`, the context's by default).

### `document_set_markdown`

```odin
document_set_markdown :: proc(doc: ^Document, md: string)
```

document_set_markdown replaces the document with the blocks `md` describes (no undo entry).

### `Markdown_Line`

```odin
Markdown_Line :: struct {
	meta:  Block_Meta,
	body:  int,  // byte where the inline text starts
	fence: bool, // a ``` line (opens or closes a code block)
	rule:  bool,
	lang:  string, // a fence's language, borrowed from the line
	alt:   string, // an image's alt text, borrowed from the line
}
```

Markdown_Line is what one source line means: its block meta, where the body starts (after "# ", "- ", "> " …), and whether it is a fence or a rule.

### `markdown_line`

```odin
markdown_line :: proc(line: string) -> (out: Markdown_Line)
```

markdown_line reads a line's block prefix (outside a fence).

### `Markdown_Emit`

```odin
Markdown_Emit :: #type proc(ctx: rawptr, piece: string, attrs: Attrs, href: string, is_mark: bool)
```

Markdown_Emit receives the pieces of a line in order: text with its style, or a mark (`**`, a link's "](url)" …) with `is_mark`.

### `markdown_scan`

```odin
markdown_scan :: proc(src: string, base: Attrs, base_href: string, ctx: rawptr, emit: Markdown_Emit)
```

markdown_scan walks the inline marks of `src` (one line, its block prefix already removed) and reports every piece. An emphasis mark opens when the next character is not a space and closes when the previous one is not (so snake_case and 2 * 3 stay text); a link's label is scanned with the link on.

### `Live_Line`

```odin
Live_Line :: struct {
	meta: Block_Meta,
	text: string,
	runs: []Run,
	src:  []int, // display byte → source byte; src[len(text)] is the source length
	in_fence: bool, // the line sits inside a code block
	fence:    bool, // the line is a fence: the opening one, or (in_fence) the closing one
}
```

Live_Line is one source line as the live-preview editor shows it: the text on screen, its style runs, and for every display byte the source byte it came from (`src[len(text)]` is the source length).

### `markdown_render_line`

```odin
markdown_render_line :: proc(line: string, in_fence: bool, keep_marks: bool, allocator := context.temp_allocator) -> (out: Live_Line)
```

markdown_render_line lays a source line out for display: block prefix and marks dropped (`keep_marks = false`, the block not being edited) or kept and styled as marks (`keep_marks = true`, the caret's block). Lines inside a fence are code. Everything is allocated with `allocator`.

## language.odin

Language describes a programming language to the code editor: the words to colour and the shapes of comments, strings, numbers and attributes. A data-driven state machine (`tokenize_line`) is enough for most languages — comments and strings carry across lines through `state`. Apps add a language by filling a Language (see LANG_SQL for a case-insensitive one); a language the table cannot describe supplies its own `tokenize` proc and keeps everything else (colours, bracket matching, comment toggle). LANG_ODIN, LANG_C, LANG_JSON, LANG_SQL and LANG_PLAIN ship.

```odin
LANG_LUA := baldr.Language{name = "Lua", keywords = {"function", "local", "end", …},
	constants = {"true", "false", "nil"}, line_comment = "--", block_comment = {"--[[", "]]"},
	quotes = "\"'", brackets = "()[]{}"}
baldr.code_editor(ui, &s.doc, &LANG_LUA)
```

### `Token_Kind`

```odin
Token_Kind :: enum u8 {
	Plain, Keyword, Type, Ident, Number, String, Comment, Operator, Punct, Constant, Attribute,
}
```

Token_Kind is the class a `Language` gives a run of code, which the code editor turns into a colour from the theme rather than one of its own.

### `Token`

```odin
Token :: struct {
	start, end: int,
	kind:       Token_Kind,
}
```

Token is a coloured run of one line: [start, end) bytes.

### `Language`

```odin
Language :: struct {
	name:           string,
	keywords:       []string,
	types:          []string,
	constants:      []string,   // true, false, nil …
	line_comment:   string,     // "//", "#", "--"; "" = none
	block_comment:  [2]string,  // "/*", "*/"; "" = none. Nesting follows `nested_comments`
	nested_comments: bool,
	quotes:         string,     // each rune opens a string closed by the same rune (escapes with '\\')
	raw_quote:      rune,       // a multi-line raw string delimiter ('`'); 0 = none
	attribute:      string,     // a prefix that starts an attribute / directive: "@" (Odin), "#" (C)
	brackets:       string,     // pairs, opener then closer: "()[]{}"
	case_insensitive: bool,     // keywords match in any case (SQL)
	tokenize:       Tokenizer,  // a custom tokenizer replaces the built-in machine (nil = built-in)
}
```

Language describes a language to `code_editor`: its name, its keyword, type and constant lists, and how it delimits comments and strings. The app owns it and the slices in it, and they must outlive the view. Without one, a `code_editor` offers the identifiers already in the document.

### `Tokenizer`

```odin
Tokenizer :: #type proc(lang: ^Language, line: string, state: u32, out: ^[dynamic]Token) -> u32
```

Tokenizer is a custom line tokenizer: append `line`'s tokens to `out` and return the state the next line starts in (0 at the top of a file).

### `LINE_IN_COMMENT`

```odin
LINE_IN_COMMENT :: u32(1)
```

Line state at a block's start: 0 = code, LINE_IN_COMMENT + depth = inside a block comment (depth − 1 nested), LINE_IN_RAW = inside a raw string.

### `tokenize_line`

```odin
tokenize_line :: proc(lang: ^Language, line: string, state: u32, out: ^[dynamic]Token) -> u32
```

tokenize_line appends the tokens of `line` to `out` and returns the state the next line starts in. Whitespace is left out (it is Plain).

### `bracket_match_in`

```odin
bracket_match_in :: proc(lang: ^Language, text: string, off: int) -> int
```

bracket_match_in finds the bracket paired with the one at `off` in `text` (a single block), by nesting; -1 when there is none.

## widget_text_input.odin

text_input: a single-line editable field over an app-owned Text buffer.

The node is a leaf of kind Text_Input_Kind. Its editing state (caret, selection anchor, horizontal scroll, blink clock, the string actually drawn) lives in the Tree side table `inputs`, so the hot node stays small and the state survives reconciliation and keyed moves. The app's Msg-typed handlers live beside it in Ui.input_handlers.

Everything is byte offsets into the buffer, always on a grapheme boundary (text_edit.odin). Caret x positions and click hit-testing use the same runa line layout render_text draws with, so the caret sits exactly between the glyphs it is between. Without a Text_Engine (headless default) the mono measurer's fixed advance is used instead, so the widget still lays out, paints, and hit-tests deterministically.

### `Text_Input_Kind`

```odin
Text_Input_Kind :: struct {}
```

Text_Input_Kind marks a text_input node. The data is in Tree.inputs.

### `TEXT_INPUT_MIN_WIDTH`

```odin
TEXT_INPUT_MIN_WIDTH :: f32(96)
```

TEXT_INPUT_MIN_WIDTH is the narrowest a field goes when a row runs out of room (a few characters stay visible; the row's other children shrink).

### `TEXT_INPUT_DEFAULT_WIDTH`

```odin
TEXT_INPUT_DEFAULT_WIDTH :: f32(240)
```

TEXT_INPUT_DEFAULT_WIDTH is the field's intrinsic width (logical px) when neither `width` nor `flex` nor the parent's stretch decides it.

### `CARET_BLINK_MS`

```odin
CARET_BLINK_MS :: 530.0
```

CARET_BLINK_MS is the caret's half-period: visible for this long, then hidden for this long, while the field is focused and idle.

### `MULTI_CLICK_MS`

```odin
MULTI_CLICK_MS :: 400.0
```

MULTI_CLICK_MS / MULTI_CLICK_PX: a press this soon and this close to the previous one counts as a double (then triple) click.

### `PASSWORD_BULLET`

```odin
PASSWORD_BULLET :: "•"
```

PASSWORD_BULLET replaces every grapheme cluster when password = true.

### `Text_Input_State`

struct

Text_Input_State is the per-field editing state (Tree.inputs side table).

### `Key_Press`

```odin
Key_Press :: struct {
	key:  Key,
	mods: Mods,
}
```

Key_Press is a key a text field passed on to its app through `on_key`, with the modifiers held.

### `Text_Handlers`

```odin
Text_Handlers :: struct($Msg: typeid) {
	on_change, on_submit: Handler(Msg, string),
	on_cancel: Maybe(Msg),              // Escape
	on_key:    Handler(Msg, Key_Press), // keys the field does not use (key_offered): a list below it walked and acted on
}
```

Text_Handlers are a field's app handlers (Ui.input_handlers side table).

### `text_input`

```odin
text_input :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), value: ^Text, placeholder: string = "", on_change: Handler(Msg, string) = nil,
	on_submit: Handler(Msg, string) = nil, label: string = "", width: f32 = 0, flex: f32 = 0, disabled := false,
	password := false, initial: string = "", compact := false, quiet := false, on_cancel: Maybe(Msg) = nil, on_key: Handler(Msg, Key_Press) = nil,
	on_paste_other: Handler(Msg, Paste_Other) = nil, max_length: int = 0,
	on_context: Handler(Msg, Editor_Context) = nil, paste_prefer: Paste_Prefer = .Text,
	tabular := false,
	focus := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

text_input is a single-line text field editing `value` in place. Typing queues `on_change` with the new contents; Enter queues `on_submit`. Keyboard: Left/Right by grapheme, Ctrl+Left/Right by word, Home/End, Shift extends the selection, Ctrl+A/C/X/V, Backspace/Delete (Ctrl: by word), Escape clears the selection. Pointer: click places the caret, drag selects, double-click selects a word, triple-click all. `label` is the accessible name (else the placeholder is used; the lint flags a field with neither). `password` draws bullets but edits the real buffer, never copies or cuts to the clipboard, and makes the buffer `secret`. `width` fixes the width; `flex` shares the parent's free space; otherwise the field stretches to its parent or takes TEXT_INPUT_DEFAULT_WIDTH. `focus` takes the keyboard focus when it turns true (a path bar opening, a filter shortcut) with the value selected, so the first thing typed replaces it and Right or End keeps it to edit instead. `on_cancel` is what Escape sends; `on_key` hears the keys a field does not use, with their modifiers, so a list below it can be walked and acted on: Up, Down, Page Up and Page Down, Shift+Delete (without `on_key` it deletes), Enter with a modifier, and Ctrl or Alt with a character key other than Ctrl+A, C, X, V, Z and Y. A Ctrl or Alt chord the window binds with `shortcut` stays the window's, as do Alt and Super with an arrow or Page key.  A plain Enter is always `on_submit`'s; with Shift, Ctrl or Alt it goes to `on_key`, since `Ev(string)` carries no modifiers (a find bar's next vs previous match). Without `on_key` a modified Enter submits; one the window bound with `shortcut` is the window's either way. `on_paste_other` hears a Ctrl+V that found no text but a picture or files on the clipboard, so the field can attach them; a paste with text is untouched. A nil `value` gives the field a buffer of its own, seeded with `initial` on its first build (a form that only wants the text on submit; the inline editors); `compact` is the tight look of an editor standing in for a label.

### `text_input_state`

```odin
text_input_state :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node) -> ^Text_Input_State
```

text_input_state returns a field's editing state (nil for any other node). Read-only for callers; tests use it to inspect the caret and selection. The pointer is into the tree's own storage: good until the next build.

### `text_input_selection`

```odin
text_input_selection :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node) -> (lo, hi: int)
```

text_input_selection returns the selected byte range [lo, hi) of a field.

### `text_input_select_all`

```odin
text_input_select_all :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node)
```

text_input_select_all selects the whole text of a field, so the next typing replaces it. Every field that commits a value on Enter (number, hex) does this to show the commit; focus stays, Tab still moves on.

### `text_input_set_selection`

```odin
text_input_set_selection :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node, anchor, caret: int)
```

text_input_set_selection selects from `anchor` to `caret` (the end typing goes at); pass one offset twice to move the caret with nothing selected. Byte offsets into the field's value, clamped and snapped to grapheme boundaries; the sorted pair from `text_input_selection` restores a selection. Focus does not move, so a keypad beside a field can place the caret without taking the keyboard; the next paint scrolls the caret into view and the accessibility bus hears that it moved. Moving the caret is not editing, so a disabled field takes this too.  Call it (and `text_input_insert`) from `view`, not `update`, which has no tree: record the key press in app state and act on it in the `view` of the same wake-up.

### `text_input_insert`

```odin
text_input_insert :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, text: string) -> bool
```

text_input_insert puts `text` into the field `n` at the caret, exactly as typing it would: what is selected is replaced, the caret ends up after it, `max_length` caps it on a grapheme boundary, control characters and newlines are dropped, `on_change` fires, and the edit joins the field's undo journal. False when `n` is no field, or is disabled.  Use it for a keypad, a palette or an emoji button rather than editing the `Text` directly: an edit the journal never saw makes Ctrl+Z restore a state that never existed. Each call is its own undo step, never coalesced with typing. An empty `text` deletes the selection, which is how a keypad's Backspace is written (select the grapheme before the caret with `text_input_set_selection`, then insert nothing); an input method's empty commit, by contrast, is dropped.

### `text_input_free`

```odin
text_input_free :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node)
```

text_input_free drops the side-table state of a node being freed.

### `text_inputs_destroy`

```odin
text_inputs_destroy :: proc(t: ^Tree)
```

text_inputs_destroy frees every field's state with the tree.

### `text_input_wake`

```odin
text_input_wake :: proc(t: ^Tree)
```

text_input_wake is called by the run loop when a requested wake fires: the focused field repaints so its caret blink advances.

### `text_event`

```odin
text_event :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), text: string) -> bool
```

text_event commits typed text (platform Ev_Text) into the focused field: it replaces the selection, or inserts at the caret. Control characters are dropped. Returns true when anything changed.

### `text_input_key`

```odin
text_input_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

text_input_key handles a key press for the focused field. Returns true if the key was consumed; Tab (and anything the field does not use) falls through to key_event.

### `text_input_pointer_button`

```odin
text_input_pointer_button :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), pressed: bool, pos: [2]f32) -> bool
```

text_input_pointer_button handles a left press or release for fields: a press focuses the field and places the caret (double-click selects the word, triple-click everything), a release ends a drag. Returns true when the event belonged to a field.

### `text_input_pointer_move`

```odin
text_input_pointer_move :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), pos: [2]f32) -> bool
```

text_input_pointer_move extends the selection while a press drags inside a field. Returns true while a drag is in progress.

## widget_text_area.odin

text_area is a multiline editor over an app-owned Document (document.odin): paragraphs wrap to the width, the area grows with its content between `min_lines` and `max_lines` (0 = unbounded) or scrolls when given a height, and everything a text field does works across lines: typing, Enter, Backspace/Delete by grapheme or word (joining blocks), arrows (Up/Down keep a goal x across wrapped lines and blocks), Home/End of the line and Ctrl+Home/End of the document, Page Up/Down, Shift selection, Ctrl+A/C/X/V, Ctrl+Z / Ctrl+Y / Ctrl+Shift+Z undo and redo (typing groups into one entry until a pause), click / drag / double-click (word) / triple-click (paragraph). Accessibility: role Text_Input, name = `label` (else the placeholder), the contents as the value, fully keyboard-operable with the standard focus ring. Tab moves focus (a text area is not a code editor). `on_change` fires with the document version after every edit; Ctrl+Enter sends `on_submit`, or Enter itself where the app says so (`editor_options(submit = .Enter)`: a chat composer, where Shift+Enter breaks the line instead). `on_context` (every editor has it) is sent on a right-click or the Menu key with an Editor_Context: where in the Document, and where to open the menu; editor_action does what the menu's Cut, Copy, Paste, Select All, Undo and Redo ask. `max_length` caps the characters (typing and pasting stop at the limit), `readonly` allows navigation, selection and copy but no edits, `invalid` / `required` colour the border and set the accessibility states, `wrap = false` keeps each paragraph on one line and scrolls sideways (code), and `marks` are ranges to decorate: Error and Warning squiggles, an Info underline, a Highlight behind the text — the hooks a spell checker, a search or a linter needs.

The code editor (widget_code.odin) is the same engine in mono with `Code_State` switched on: it adds a gutter, tab stops, syntax colour, indentation keys, bracket matching, find and go-to-line — nothing here is duplicated there. Painting is virtual: only the blocks in view are shaped and drawn, from a cache of block tops kept per document version.

### `Mark`

```odin
Mark :: struct {
	from, to: Pos,
	kind:     Mark_Kind,
	message:  string,
	color:    Color, // .Tint's colour; ignored by the others
}
```

Mark decorates a document range: what a spell checker, a search or a linter wants to show. Marks are copied per build (pass the current list). A `message` shows in a tooltip when the pointer rests on the mark (a diagnostic's text, a suggestion); the string must outlive the build. A mark is display only: never in the document, copied or exported. A new list every build is cheap, which is what lets a fade follow the caret.

### `Mark_Kind`

```odin
Mark_Kind :: enum u8 { Error, Warning, Info, Highlight, Dim, Tint, Strike }
```

Mark_Kind is how a mark shows. The first four draw around the text (a wave, a rule, a wash); the last three change how the text itself is drawn:  - Dim: the theme's muted text colour (sentence focus: may cover most of a document and move with every caret press). - Tint: the mark's own `color` (parts of speech); where marks overlap, the last one wins. - Strike: a line through the words in the warning colour (a style check's "consider cutting this").

### `Submit_Key`

```odin
Submit_Key :: enum u8 { Ctrl_Enter, Enter }
```

Submit_Key is which Enter sends a text area's `on_submit`: Ctrl+Enter (the default, for a notes field, where Enter breaks the line), or Enter itself, with Shift+Enter for the line break — what every chat composer does. An input method's Enter always commits its composition instead.

### `REVEAL_PASSES`

```odin
REVEAL_PASSES :: 4
```

REVEAL_PASSES is how many times a reveal may scroll, measure what it landed on and look again before settling for where it is. Two is the ordinary case; a jump to the end of a long file takes three.

### `Reveal_At`

```odin
Reveal_At :: enum u8 {
	Nearest,
	Center,
	Top,
}
```

Reveal_At is where editor_reveal puts a position in view: with as little scrolling as shows it, in the middle, or on the top line (a view put back where editor_view said it was).

### `Table_Cached`

```odin
Table_Cached :: struct {
	cols:    []f32,
	version: u64,
	wrap_w:  f32,
	size:    f32,
	raw_key: int, // which blocks were raw when measured (live: the pipes' widths differ)
}
```

Table_Cached: one table's column starts (tree allocator).

### `Live_Cached`

```odin
Live_Cached :: struct {
	line:    Live_Line,
	stamp:   u64, // the block's own stamp, for the reason Rich_Cached gives
	raw:     bool,
}
```

Live_Cached is one source line rendered for display (tree allocator).

### `Rich_Cached`

```odin
Rich_Cached :: struct {
	lay:     Rich_Layout,
	// The block's own stamp, not the document's version: one letter typed
	// bumps the whole document's version, and keying on it would re-lay
	// every block on every keystroke.
	stamp:   u64,
	wrap_w:  f32,
	size:    f32,
	text_len: int, // a live block's display text changes with the caret, not the version
	raw:      bool,
	family:   Family_Id, // the family it was laid out in
}
```

Rich_Cached is one block's rich layout, kept until the document or the width changes.

### `Line_Span`

```odin
Line_Span :: struct {
	start, end: int,
	width:      f32,
}
```

Line_Span is one wrapped line of a block: [start, end) bytes and its width.

### `AREA_LINE_LIMIT`

```odin
AREA_LINE_LIMIT :: 10_000
```

AREA_LINE_LIMIT is how much of one line an editor lays out and draws, in bytes, unless the app says otherwise (`editor_options(line_limit = …)`). Laying a line out costs about 650 bytes a character, so a line of millions (a minified script, an inlined base64 picture) would cost gigabytes. Past the limit a marker says how much more there is; the document keeps the whole line, so copy, save and find see all of it.

### `text_area`

```odin
text_area :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), doc: ^Document, placeholder: string = "", on_change: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	on_submit: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, on_context: Handler(Msg, Editor_Context) = nil, label: string = "", width: f32 = 0,
	height: f32 = 0, flex: f32 = 0, disabled := false, focus := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `editor_options`

```odin
editor_options :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, wrap: Maybe(bool) = nil, readonly: Maybe(bool) = nil, submit: Maybe(Submit_Key) = nil,
	min_lines: Maybe(int) = nil, max_lines: Maybe(int) = nil, max_length: Maybe(int) = nil,
	line_numbers: Maybe(bool) = nil,
	tab_width: Maybe(int) = nil, spaces: Maybe(bool) = nil, whitespace: Maybe(bool) = nil, ligatures: Maybe(bool) = nil, toolbar: Maybe(bool) = nil,
	font: Maybe(string) = nil, size: Maybe(f32) = nil, line_spacing: Maybe(f32) = nil, frame: Maybe(bool) = nil,
	ink: Maybe(Color) = nil, on_paste_other: Handler(Msg, Paste_Other) = nil,
	paste_prefer: Maybe(Paste_Prefer) = nil,
	follow_caret: Maybe(bool) = nil, line_limit: Maybe(int) = nil)
```

editor_options sets how an editor built as `n` (a text area, code editor or rich editor — the node the builder returned) behaves. Each setting stays until changed, so pass only what you mean:  ed := baldr.code_editor(ui, &s.src, &baldr.LANG_ODIN, on_change, flex = 1) baldr.editor_options(ui, ed, wrap = true, whitespace = true, tab_width = 2, spaces = true)  `wrap` soft-wraps lines (a text area's and rich editor's default; a code editor scrolls sideways), `readonly` allows navigation and copying but no edits, `min_lines` / `max_lines` bound the height when none is given (0 = unbounded), `max_length` caps the characters (0 = unlimited), `invalid` / `required` colour the border and set the accessibility states; for code editors `line_numbers`, `tab_width`, `spaces` (indent with spaces), `whitespace` (show it) and `ligatures` (off by default: "->" stays two glyphs); for rich editors `toolbar`.  `frame = false` is for an editor that fills a window: no border, no focus colour, square corners and no fill, so the page does not look like a form field (the caret says where focus is).  `on_paste_other` is for a composer that takes more than text: Ctrl+V (or the menu's Paste) with a picture or files and no text on the clipboard sends it `Paste_Other`, so a pasted screenshot can become an attachment. A paste that brought text is untouched.  `line_limit` is how much of one line is laid out and drawn, in bytes: AREA_LINE_LIMIT unless set, 0 for no limit. Past it a muted marker says how many more characters there are, and the document keeps them all; a live-markdown editor shows such a line as its source.  How the document is shown, rather than what is in it: `font` is an installed family (installed_fonts lists them; the toolkit's own fonts stay behind it for anything the family lacks, and editor_font says whether it was found), `size` the body size in logical px that the editor's other sizes scale from, and `line_spacing` a multiplier on the line height (1.5 for double-spaced prose). A change re-lays the document; the caret, the selection and the view stay where they are. "" and 0 go back to the theme's font and size.

### `editor_control`

```odin
editor_control :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, editor: Node)
```

editor_control counts `n` — a field or button of the app's own — as part of the editor built as `editor`, the way an editor's built-in find bar is part of it: while focus is in `n`, the editor keeps showing its selection, so an app's own find bar can select each match and have it look selected. It stays until the node goes; pass NODE_NONE as `editor` to undo it.

### `editor_font`

```odin
editor_font :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node) -> (family: string, installed: bool)
```

editor_font is the family an editor built as `n` draws its document in: the name as this machine has it, and whether the family the app asked for was found; the name aliases the text engine's own storage. An app that saved "EB Garamond" with a document can tell the reader it is not installed any more instead of quietly drawing something else. "" with `installed` false means it draws in the theme's own font.

### `editor_select`

```odin
editor_select :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, anchor, caret: Pos)
```

editor_select sets the selection of a text area, code editor, rich or markdown editor built as `n` (its frame or the area), from `anchor` to `caret` — just the caret when they are the same: an outline jumping to a heading, find selecting its current match, the caret put after text an app replaced. Positions are clamped to its Document, the column Up and Down keep is reset as a click resets it, and the caret is kept in view once the editor is laid out. editor_caret reads it back.

### `editor_reveal`

```odin
editor_reveal :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, pos: Pos, at: Reveal_At = .Nearest)
```

editor_reveal scrolls the editor built as `n` so that `pos` is in view: with as little movement as that takes (`.Nearest`), in the middle (`.Center`: a heading jumped to, a match found), or on the top line (`.Top`: where editor_view said the view was, put back when a file is reopened). It waits for the editor's layout, so it can be called straight after building it or editing its Document; the last call before that layout is the one that counts. Positions are clamped to the Document.

### `editor_caret`

```odin
editor_caret :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node) -> (caret, anchor: Pos, ok: bool)
```

editor_caret reads the caret of the editor built as `n`, and the end it was dragged or shifted from (`anchor == caret` when nothing is selected). Not ok for a node that is not an editor. It reads what `editor_select` writes, in the same order; `editor_selection` gives the range ordered instead. Use this one when the direction matters: sentence focus, a status bar's line and column. Pair it with `editor_options(follow_caret = true)` to be rebuilt when the caret moves, since a caret move alone only repaints.

### `editor_view`

```odin
editor_view :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node) -> (top: Pos, ok: bool)
```

editor_view is where the editor built as `n` is scrolled to: the first position on the line at the top of its view, for editor_reveal(…, .Top) to put the view back later. Not ok for an editor not laid out yet.

### `Editor_Context`

```odin
Editor_Context :: struct {
	pos:          Pos,
	in_selection: bool,
	at:           [2]f32,
}
```

Editor_Context is what an editor's `on_context` carries: the Document position a right-click landed on (the caret, for the Menu key or Shift+F10), whether that is inside the selection, and where to open the menu, in window coordinates: under the pointer, or under the caret. Ui.context_pos is set to `at` first, so context_menu opens there. A right-click outside the selection has already moved the caret to `pos`; inside it the selection stays, for Cut and Copy.

### `Editor_Action`

```odin
Editor_Action :: enum u8 {
	Cut, Copy, Paste, Select_All, Undo, Redo,
}
```

Editor_Action is what an app's menu asks an editor to do: what Ctrl+X, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+V, Ctrl+A, Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Shift+Z (or Ctrl+Y) do.

### `Editor_Actions`

```odin
Editor_Actions :: bit_set[Editor_Action; u8]
```

Editor_Actions is a set of Editor_Action: what editor_actions says an editor can do now.

### `editor_action`

```odin
editor_action :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, action: Editor_Action) -> bool
```

editor_action does `action` in the editor built as `n` (its frame or the area) exactly as its shortcut does: the same clipboard, the same undo steps, the caret and the view following. Focus stays where it is. False when there was nothing to do: no selection to cut or copy, no text on the clipboard, no step to undo or redo, an empty Document to select, a readonly editor asked to cut, paste, undo or redo, a disabled editor.

### `editor_actions`

```odin
editor_actions :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node) -> (can: Editor_Actions)
```

editor_actions is what editor_action can do in the editor built as `n` now, to grey a menu's items: Cut and Copy need a selection, Paste text on the clipboard, Select All some text, Undo and Redo a step to take; Cut, Paste, Undo and Redo an editor that is not readonly. Empty for a disabled editor or a node that is none.

### `editor_marks`

```odin
editor_marks :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, marks: []Mark, loc := #caller_location)
```

editor_marks decorates ranges of an editor's document for this build: squiggles, underlines and highlights from a spell checker, a search or a linter (see Mark). Pass the current list every build.

### `WIDEST_CANDIDATES`

```odin
WIDEST_CANDIDATES :: 8
```

WIDEST_CANDIDATES is how many of the longest lines are shaped to find how wide an unwrapped editor's content is. Byte length ranks them and shaping settles it: the longest line in bytes is not always the widest drawn (a line of commas against a line of Ws), so a handful are tried rather than one.

### `WIDEST_INDENT_SCAN`

```odin
WIDEST_INDENT_SCAN :: 64
```

WIDEST_INDENT_SCAN is how far into a line the ranking looks for the tabs that make it wider than its byte count says. Indentation lives at the front, and reading every byte of every line would be the whole-document cost this ranking exists to avoid.

### `MARK_SPAN_SHORT`

```odin
MARK_SPAN_SHORT :: 1 // blocks a mark may cover and still be found by its first
```

------------------------------------------------------- marks by block  An editor hands over its marks every build, and a spell checker's are one a word: hundreds of thousands in a large file. Walking the list per block in view is too slow, so it is indexed when it changes: marks within a block or two are sorted by where they start, and the few that cover more are kept aside and always looked at. Painting a block then costs the marks on it, not the document's.

### `text_area_text`

```odin
text_area_text :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), text: string) -> bool
```

text_area_text commits typed text into the focused area.

### `text_area_key`

```odin
text_area_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

### `text_area_pointer_button`

```odin
text_area_pointer_button :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), pressed: bool, pos: [2]f32) -> bool
```

### `text_area_pointer_move`

```odin
text_area_pointer_move :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), pos: [2]f32) -> bool
```

## widget_code.odin

code_editor is the text area engine in the code face, for source: a line-number gutter (click one to select the line), tab stops, syntax colour from a `Language` (Odin, C, JSON ship; apps add their own), the caret line washed, matching brackets boxed, whitespace shown on request, wrap off by default (on request it soft-wraps). Editing keys on top of the text area's: Tab indents (the selected lines, or inserts one indent), Shift+Tab outdents, Enter keeps the indentation and opens a bracket pair onto its own line, Home goes to the first non-blank column then the margin, Ctrl+/ toggles line comments, Alt+Up / Down move lines, Ctrl+F opens find (Enter / Shift+Enter or F3 / Shift+F3 step through the matches, Escape closes and returns focus), Ctrl+G goes to a line, Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab move focus out (Tab is taken). Painting is virtual, so a 50 000-line file scrolls at full rate. Accessibility: Text_Input named `label` with the text as its value and "Line n, column m" as its description; the find bar is a field "Find" with buttons "Previous match", "Next match", "Close find".

```odin
baldr.code_editor(ui, &s.source, &baldr.LANG_ODIN, on_change = proc(e: baldr.Ev(int)) -> Msg { return Edited{} }, height = 400)
```

### `Code_Bar`

```odin
Code_Bar :: enum u8 { None, Find, Replace, Goto }
```

Code_Bar is which of a code editor's own bars is open: find, find and replace, or go-to-line.

### `Code_State`

struct

Code_State: the code editor's extras on a Text_Area_State.

### `code_editor`

```odin
code_editor :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), doc: ^Document, language: ^Language = nil, on_change: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	on_context: Handler(Msg, Editor_Context) = nil, label: string = "Code", width: f32 = 0, height: f32 = 0, flex: f32 = 0, disabled := false, focus := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0,
	loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `code_bar_key`

```odin
code_bar_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

code_bar_key: keys in the bar's field — Enter steps / jumps, Escape closes.

### `code_tick`

```odin
code_tick :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg))
```

code_tick asks for a rebuild when a find field's text no longer matches what the matches were computed for (typing in the field does not rebuild on its own).

### `CODE_MATCH_SPAN`

```odin
CODE_MATCH_SPAN :: 2000
```

code_bracket_match finds the mate of the bracket at `p` across blocks, counting nesting; up to CODE_MATCH_SPAN blocks away.

### `default_completer`

```odin
default_completer :: proc(doc: ^Document, at: Pos, prefix: string, lang: ^Language, out: ^[dynamic]Completion)
```

default_completer: the language's keywords, types and constants, and the identifiers of the surrounding CODE_COMPLETE_SCAN lines, that start with the prefix (the word being typed itself excluded).

## widget_rich.odin

rich_text is the text area engine with the document's kinds and runs switched on: headings, bullet / numbered / task lists (nested), quotes, code blocks and rules, with bold, italic, underline, strike, inline code and links inside. A toolbar above reflects the selection and applies formatting; the keys do the same (Ctrl+B / I / U, Ctrl+E code, Ctrl+K link); typing markdown at the start of a line converts it ("# ", "- ", "1. ", "[ ] ", "> ", "```"); Enter on an empty list item leaves the list, Backspace at the start of a styled block makes it a paragraph, Tab / Shift+Tab nest list items. A click on a task's box ticks it; Ctrl+click on a link sends `on_link`. The Document is the app's; document_markdown / document_set_markdown move it to and from markdown. Accessibility: Text_Input named `label`, the toolbar a Toolbar of named Buttons with Pressed states, the link bar a Group.

```odin
