```odin
baldr.rich_text(ui, &s.notes, on_change = proc(e: baldr.Ev(int)) -> Msg { return Edited{} }, label = "Notes")
```

### `Image_Source`

```odin
Image_Source :: #type proc(src: string) -> ^Image
```

Image_Source resolves an image block's `src` (a path, a URL, a name) to a picture the app keeps alive; nil = not available (the alt text shows).

### `rich_tick`

```odin
rich_tick :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg))
```

rich_tick rebuilds an editor whose toolbar no longer matches its selection (a caret move does not rebuild on its own). It asks once per change: an editor inside a skipped `component` cannot rebuild, and asking again every wake-up would spin.

### `Rich_Action`

```odin
Rich_Action :: enum u8 {
	None, Bold, Italic, Underline, Strike, Code, Highlight, Link, H1, H2, H3, Paragraph,
	Bullet, Numbered, Task, Quote, Code_Block, Rule, Undo, Redo, Link_Apply, Link_Remove, Link_Close,
	Image, Image_Apply, Table,
}
```

Rich actions (toolbar buttons and keys share them).

### `editor_images`

```odin
editor_images :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, images: Image_Source)
```

editor_images gives a rich or live editor the pictures behind its image blocks (`![alt](src)` in markdown). Stays until changed.

### `rich_text`

```odin
rich_text :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), doc: ^Document, on_change: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, label: string = "Text",
	placeholder: string = "", width: f32 = 0, height: f32 = 0, flex: f32 = 0, on_link: Handler(Msg, string) = nil,
	on_context: Handler(Msg, Editor_Context) = nil, disabled := false, focus := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `rich_apply`

```odin
rich_apply :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), frame: Node, action: Rich_Action)
```

rich_apply performs a formatting action on the rich or markdown editor built as `frame` (its frame or the area) — what its toolbar and its shortcuts do — for an app's own toolbar or menu: `rich_apply(ui, editor, .Bold)`. It acts on the selection, or on the word at the caret where a mark needs one (Bold, Italic, Code, Strike, Highlight, Link), and is one undo step.  A rich editor keeps the formatting in the Document's runs and kinds; a markdown editor edits its source instead, putting `**`, `` ` ``, `==`, `#`, `- `, `> ` or ``` ``` ``` in the text, so what is saved is the markdown. Its Link and Image write `[text](url)` and `![text](url)` with the address selected, since it has no link bar, and the bar's own actions (Link_Apply, Link_Remove, Link_Close, Image_Apply) do nothing there. Focus returns to the text, as it does from the toolbar.

### `editor_selection`

```odin
editor_selection :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node) -> (lo, hi: Pos, ok: bool)
```

editor_selection is the selection of a text area, code editor or rich editor built as `n` (its frame or the area), as ordered positions in its Document; an app formats or inspects that range through the Document API.  `lo` is always before `hi`, whichever way the person dragged. When the direction matters, read `editor_caret`, which gives the caret and its anchor unswapped.

### `rich_ctl_key`

```odin
rich_ctl_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

rich_ctl_key: keys in the link bar's field.

## widget_controls.odin

Checkbox, toggle, slider. Each is a Focusable node with a role; checkbox and toggle are activated through click() (pointer or Enter/Space) and report the flipped value through their Handler; the slider maps pointer x to a value while pressed. Toggle and slider paint themselves through Custom_Kind; the toggle's knob glides with an anim value.

### `Slider_State`

struct

Slider_State is the slider's model, kept on the tree so paint (which has no Msg type) can draw it.

### `Slider_Track`

```odin
Slider_Track :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node, f: f32) -> Color
```

Slider_Track samples the colour of a slider's track at fraction `f` (0..1).

### `Slider_Size`

```odin
Slider_Size :: enum u8 { Regular, Large }
```

Slider_Size: Regular is the thin track with a small thumb; Large a pill as tall as its round knob, the accent filling it, with an icon inside the filled start (a quick-settings volume or brightness).

### `checkbox`

```odin
checkbox :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), checked: bool, label: string, on_change: Handler(Msg, bool) = nil,
	disabled := false, hide_label := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

checkbox is a labelled on/off control. `on_change` receives the new value. `hide_label` keeps `label` as its name without drawing it.

### `toggle`

```odin
toggle :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), on: bool, label: string = "", on_change: Handler(Msg, bool) = nil,
	disabled := false, hide_label := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

toggle is an on/off switch with a gliding knob, its `label` beside it and naming it. `hide_label` keeps the name without drawing it (a page header whose title already says it).

### `icon_toggle`

```odin
icon_toggle :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), icon: Icon, on: bool, label: string, on_change: Handler(Msg, bool) = nil,
	size: f32 = 0, disabled := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

icon_toggle is a round on/off button showing `icon`: a quick-settings tile's Wi-Fi or Do Not Disturb. On it is the accent with the icon in the on-accent colour, off the strong fill with the icon in the content colour, easing between them; `size` is its diameter (the theme's icon_toggle.size when 0). A click, Enter or Space flips it and `on_change` receives the new value. A Toggle_Button named by `label` (the tile's title), Pressed while on; the words beside or under it are the app's.

### `slider`

```odin
slider :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), value: f32, min: f32 = 0, max: f32 = 1, on_change: Handler(Msg, f32) = nil,
	step: f32 = 0, width: f32 = 0, flex: f32 = 0, size: Slider_Size = .Regular, icon: Icon = .None, label: string = "", disabled := false,
	on_icon: Maybe(Msg) = nil, icon_label: string = "", icon_pressed := false, on_commit: Handler(Msg, f32) = nil,
	focus_on_click := true, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

slider picks a number in [min, max]; `on_change` receives the value while dragging (snapped to `step` when > 0). `size = .Large` makes it a pill as tall as its round knob, and `icon` shows inside its filled start (pass another with the value: a muted speaker at 0). `on_icon` makes a Large slider's icon a toggle of its own (a mute): a Toggle_Button named `icon_label`, Pressed while `icon_pressed`, a Tab stop before the track. A click, Enter or Space on it sends `on_icon`; a press on the track beside it still sets the value. Returns the slider itself.

### `slider_options`

```odin
slider_options :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, marks: []f32 = nil, format: proc(v: f32) -> string = nil)
```

slider_options sets a slider's rarer parts, passed every build: `marks`, values ticked along its track (an audiobook's chapters, bookmarks), which Ctrl + Left / Right step between; and `format`, the value as a reader reads it ("3:25" rather than 205) — shown above the thumb while it is dragged and above the pointer while it rests on the track, and given to assistive technology as the value's text. `format` returns the frame's temp memory; what it says is kept.  s := baldr.slider(ui, pos, 0, dur, on_change = Scrub, on_commit = Seek, label = "Position") baldr.slider_options(ui, s, marks = chapter_starts[:], format = clock_text)

### `SLIDER_HOLD_MS`

```odin
SLIDER_HOLD_MS :: 1000.0
```

SLIDER_HOLD_MS is the longest a committed value stands against the app's.

### `slider_value_at`

```odin
slider_value_at :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node, x: f32) -> (f32, bool)
```

slider_value_at maps a pointer x to the slider's value (snapped to step).

### `slider_pointer`

```odin
slider_pointer :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), n: Node, pos: [2]f32) -> bool
```

slider_pointer handles a press or drag at `pos`: updates the model, paints, and sends on_change when the value changed. Returns true if consumed.

### `slider_key`

```odin
slider_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

slider_key: Left / Down and Right / Up move by `step` (a hundredth of the range when there is none), Page Up / Down by a tenth, Home / End to the ends.

## widget_choice.odin

radio + segmented: one pick among a few. Both are a single Tab stop — the arrow keys move the pick (Home / End to the ends, wrapping) and send on_change; a click picks; the picked item shows the ring. radio lists options with a dot, down the page (or `horizontal`); segmented is a pill row whose picked segment glides. The pick is the app's state. Accessibility: a Radio_Group named `label` of Radios with the Checked state.

```odin
baldr.radio(ui, s.fruit, FRUITS, proc(e: baldr.Ev(int)) -> Msg { return Set_Fruit{e.value} }, label = "Fruit")
baldr.segmented(ui, s.density, {"Compact", "Comfortable", "Spacious"}, on_change = …, label = "Density")
```

### `radio`

```odin
radio :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), selected: int, options: []string, on_change: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, label: string = "",
	horizontal := false, disabled := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `segmented`

```odin
segmented :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), selected: int, labels: []string, on_change: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, icons: []Icon = nil, label: string = "",
	width: f32 = 0, flex: f32 = 0, disabled := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

segmented is a pill row of segments, one picked. It sizes to its labels; give it a `width` (or `flex` in a row) to spread the segments across it: equal while each fits an equal share; else the ones that need more keep their width and the rest share what is left alike; then their padding tightens alike; only then are the widest labels cut to one width, ending in "…" (the whole label stays the segment's name). `icons`, one per segment (.None to skip one), go before the labels in the labels' colour (a power mode's leaf, gauge and bolt).

### `choice_key`

```odin
choice_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

choice_key moves a focused group's pick with the arrows, Home and End.

## widget_number.odin

number_input: a field for a number with − / + buttons. Up / Down step it (Shift: ten steps), Enter commits what was typed, Escape puts the value back, and leaving the field commits too; the value is clamped to `min` / `max` and shown with as many decimals as `step` has. The value is the app's state; on_change carries the new one. Accessibility: the field is a Spin_Button named `label` with value / min / max; the buttons are "Decrease <label>" and "Increase <label>".

```odin
baldr.number_input(ui, s.count, proc(e: baldr.Ev(f32)) -> Msg { return Set_Count{e.value} }, min = 0, max = 100, label = "Count")
```

### `Spin_Step`

```odin
Spin_Step :: struct {
	outer: Node,
	dir:   int,
}
```

Spin_Step is a press on one of a number input's stepper buttons: which input, and which way.

### `number_input`

```odin
number_input :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), value: f32, on_change: Handler(Msg, f32) = nil, min: f32 = -3.4e38, max: f32 = 3.4e38,
	step: f32 = 1, label: string = "", width: f32 = 150, disabled := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `step_decimals`

```odin
step_decimals :: proc "contextless" (step: f32) -> int
```

step_decimals: how many decimals `step` needs (0.25 → 2), at most 6.

### `number_format`

```odin
number_format :: proc(t: ^Tree, v: f32, decimals: int) -> string
```

number_format writes `v` with `decimals`, using the locale's decimal mark. The text is in the frame's temp memory.

### `number_step`

```odin
number_step :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), outer: Node, dir, mult: int) -> bool
```

number_step nudges by `dir` steps (× mult) and sends on_change.

### `number_key`

```odin
number_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

number_key: Up / Down step (Shift ×10), Enter commits, Escape reverts.

## widget_date.odin

date_picker: a month grid to pick a day. The picked date is the app's state (on_change carries the new one); the month shown and the keyboard cursor are the widget's. One Tab stop: the arrows move the cursor by a day or a week (crossing into the next month turns the page), Page Up / Down turn the page, Home / End go to the week's ends, Enter / Space pick the cursor day, a click picks a day; ‹ › turn the page. Days outside `min` / `max` are disabled. Put it in a popover for a drop-down field. Accessibility: a Group named `label` of Buttons named in full ("11 September 2026"), the picked day Selected, today described.

```odin
baldr.date_picker(ui, s.date, proc(e: baldr.Ev(baldr.Date)) -> Msg { return Set_Date{e.value} })
```

### `Weekday`

```odin
Weekday :: dt.Weekday // Sunday = 0
```

Weekday is a day of the week, Sunday = 0. Which day a calendar starts on comes from the desktop's locale, not from this.

### `Week_Start`

```odin
Week_Start :: enum u8 { Locale, Sunday, Monday, Saturday }
```

Week_Start: whose week the grid starts on — the locale's, or a fixed day.

### `Cal_Nav`

```odin
Cal_Nav :: struct {
	cal:   Node,
	delta: int, // months
}
```

Cal_Nav is a press on a calendar's back or forward control, and how many months it moves.

### `today`

```odin
today :: proc() -> Date
```

today is the local date.

### `date_valid`

```odin
date_valid :: proc "contextless" (d: Date) -> bool
```

### `date_ordinal`

```odin
date_ordinal :: proc "contextless" (d: Date) -> i64
```

date_ordinal counts days since the epoch the calendar uses (0 for an invalid date).

### `date_from_ordinal`

```odin
date_from_ordinal :: proc "contextless" (o: i64) -> Date
```

### `date_add_days`

```odin
date_add_days :: proc "contextless" (d: Date, days: int) -> Date
```

### `date_add_months`

```odin
date_add_months :: proc "contextless" (d: Date, months: int) -> Date
```

date_add_months keeps the day where the month allows and clamps otherwise.

### `date_before`

```odin
date_before :: proc "contextless" (a, b: Date) -> bool
```

### `date_text`

```odin
date_text :: proc(t: ^Tree, d: Date) -> string
```

date_text writes a date in full the way the locale orders it: "11 September 2026" (en_GB), "September 11 2026" (en_US), following the locale's date format with the month spelled out. Temp allocated.

### `date_short`

```odin
date_short :: proc(t: ^Tree, d: Date) -> string
```

date_short writes a date in the locale's short order — "11/09/2026", "09/11/2026", "2026-09-11" — always with a four-digit year, even where the locale's own short form has two (a form field must not be ambiguous). Temp allocated.

### `date_format`

```odin
date_format :: proc(t: ^Tree, d: Date, long: bool) -> string
```

date_format follows the locale's strftime-style date format. `long` spells the month out and joins the parts with spaces (or the locale's own separators when they are not slashes). The text is in the frame's temp memory.

### `date_weekday_initial`

```odin
date_weekday_initial :: proc(t: ^Tree, wd: int) -> string
```

date_weekday_initial is the locale's one-letter day (Sunday = 0). It aliases the tree's locale, which changes only when the desktop's does.

### `date_picker`

```odin
date_picker :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), date: Date, on_change: Handler(Msg, Date) = nil, label: string = "Date",
	min: Date = {}, max: Date = {}, week_start: Week_Start = .Locale, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `calendar_key`

```odin
calendar_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

calendar_key moves the cursor and picks it with Enter / Space.

### `date_field`

```odin
date_field :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), date: Date, on_change: Handler(Msg, Date) = nil, label: string = "Date",
	min: Date = {}, max: Date = {}, week_start: Week_Start = .Locale, width: f32 = 170, disabled := false,
	tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

date_field is the drop-down form: a field showing the date the way the locale writes it, with a calendar icon; a click, Enter or Space opens a date_picker in a popover, a pick closes it, Escape or a press elsewhere dismisses it. Accessibility: Combo_Box named `label` with the date as its value, with a popup, Expanded while open.  baldr.date_field(ui, s.date, proc(e: baldr.Ev(baldr.Date)) -> Msg { return Set_Date{e.value} }, label = "Due")

## widget_time.odin

time_picker: one field with hour, minute, optional second and AM / PM segments. Click a segment (or Left / Right) to pick it, then type the digits, press Up / Down (Shift: ten minutes), turn the wheel, or drag up and down on it; A and P set the period; Home / End go to the ends. It is one Tab stop; the picked segment carries the ring; a click on AM / PM flips it. `clock` follows the locale unless forced to 12 or 24 hours; `seconds` shows a third segment. The time is the app's state and on_change carries the new one — a wall-clock Time_Of_Day with no zone: what it means (local, UTC) is the app's call, and core:time converts instants either way. Accessibility: a Group named `label` of Spin_Buttons "Hour", "Minute", "Second" and "AM / PM", each with its value; on touch, ▲ ▼ buttons nudge the picked segment.

```odin
baldr.time_picker(ui, s.alarm, proc(e: baldr.Ev(baldr.Time_Of_Day)) -> Msg { return Set_Alarm{e.value} })
baldr.time_field(ui, s.alarm, …, label = "Alarm")   // the drop-down form
```

### `Time_Of_Day`

```odin
Time_Of_Day :: dt.Time // hour, minute, second, nano
```

Time_Of_Day is a wall-clock time — hour, minute, second, nanosecond — with no date and no zone. It is what `time_field` and `time_picker` read and report.

### `Clock`

```odin
Clock :: enum u8 { Locale, H24, H12 }
```

Clock: how hours are shown — the locale's way, or forced.

### `Time_Seg`

```odin
Time_Seg :: enum u8 { Hour, Minute, Second, Period }
```

Time_Seg is one editable part of a time — the hours, the minutes, the seconds, or AM/PM — which is also the unit the arrow keys and a scrub move.

### `Time_Scrub`

```odin
Time_Scrub :: struct {
	picker:  Node,
	seg:     Time_Seg,
	start_y: f32,
	start:   int, // the segment's value at the press
	moved:   bool,
}
```

Time_Scrub is a drag on one segment of a time picker: where it started and what the segment read then, so the gesture is relative to the press.

### `now_local`

```odin
now_local :: proc() -> Time_Of_Day
```

now_local is the clock here — the desktop's zone, read from the OS (TZ or /etc/localtime on Unix, the registry on Windows); now_utc is the same instant in UTC. Both are what a form usually starts from.

### `time_of_day`

```odin
time_of_day :: proc(t: time.Time, local := false) -> Time_Of_Day
```

time_of_day is the clock reading of an instant, in UTC or in the desktop's zone. The zone data is read on each local call (a few small file reads on Unix, temp-allocated), so keep it out of per-frame code; an unreadable zone falls back to UTC.

### `time_text`

```odin
time_text :: proc(t: ^Tree, tm: Time_Of_Day, seconds := false, clock: Clock = .Locale) -> string
```

time_text writes a time the locale's way: "07:30", "07:30:05", "7:30 PM", in the frame's temp memory.

### `time_picker`

```odin
time_picker :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), time: Time_Of_Day, on_change: Handler(Msg, Time_Of_Day) = nil, label: string = "Time",
	clock: Clock = .Locale, seconds := false, disabled := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `time_key`

```odin
time_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

time_key: arrows and Home / End on the focused picker.

### `time_type`

```odin
time_type :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), s: string) -> bool
```

time_type: digits typed into the picked segment (two per segment, the first alone when a second could not fit), A / P for the period. Called with committed text.

### `time_tick`

```odin
time_tick :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg))
```

time_tick rebuilds a picker whose focus changed since it was built, so the picked segment's highlight follows focus (focus_first after a popover opens, a click elsewhere) without the app doing anything.

### `time_field`

```odin
time_field :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), time: Time_Of_Day, on_change: Handler(Msg, Time_Of_Day) = nil, label: string = "Time",
	clock: Clock = .Locale, seconds := false, width: f32 = 150, disabled := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

time_field is the drop-down form: shows the time the locale's way with a clock icon; click, Enter or Space open a time_picker in a popover, Enter inside it or Escape closes it. Accessibility: Combo_Box named `label` with the time as its value, with a popup, Expanded while open.

## widget_color.odin

color_picker: a swatch, a hex field and three sliders — Hue, Chroma and Lightness in OKLCH, the space the themes are built in, so every step along a slider is a perceptually even one and the tracks show exactly the colours they select. The colour is the app's state (on_change carries the new one); the sliders and the hex field are keyboard operable like any slider or field (Enter commits the hex, Escape puts it back, leaving the field commits). Accessibility: a Group named `label` of Sliders "Hue", "Chroma", "Lightness" and a field "Hex".

```odin
baldr.color_picker(ui, s.accent, proc(e: baldr.Ev(baldr.Color)) -> Msg { return Set_Accent{e.value} })
```

### `color_picker`

```odin
color_picker :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), color: Color, on_change: Handler(Msg, Color) = nil, label: string = "Colour",
	width: f32 = 280, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `picker_key`

```odin
picker_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

picker_key: Enter commits the hex field, Escape puts the colour back.

### `color_hex`

```odin
color_hex :: proc(c: Color) -> string
```

color_hex writes a Color as "#rrggbb" (sRGB), in the frame's temp memory: copy it into anything you keep.

### `color_parse_hex`

```odin
color_parse_hex :: proc(s: string) -> (c: Color, ok: bool)
```

color_parse_hex reads "#rrggbb", "rrggbb" or "#rgb".

### `color_field`

```odin
color_field :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), color: Color, on_change: Handler(Msg, Color) = nil, label: string = "Colour",
	width: f32 = 150, disabled := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

color_field is the drop-down form: a swatch and the hex, opening a color_picker in a popover on click, Enter or Space; Escape or a press elsewhere closes it. Accessibility: Combo_Box named `label` described by the hex, with a popup, Expanded while open.  baldr.color_field(ui, s.accent, proc(e: baldr.Ev(baldr.Color)) -> Msg { return Set_Accent{e.value} }, label = "Accent")

## widget_chip.odin

chip is a pill for a tag or a filter: `selected` shows a check and the tonal fill, a click sends on_change with the flipped value (Enter / Space too); `on_close` adds a small × that sends it (Backspace / Delete on a focused chip too). Accessibility: Toggle_Button named `label`, Pressed when selected; the × is a Button "Remove <label>".

```odin
baldr.chip(ui, "Wayland", selected = s.filters[i], on_change = …, tag = u64(i))
baldr.chip(ui, tag, on_close = Msg(Remove_Tag{i}))
```

### `chip`

```odin
chip :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), label: string, selected := false, on_change: Handler(Msg, bool) = nil, on_close: Maybe(Msg) = nil,
	icon: Icon = .None, disabled := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `chip_key`

```odin
chip_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key) -> bool
```

chip_key: Backspace / Delete on a focused removable chip removes it.

## widget_expander.odin

expander is a disclosure: a header row with a chevron that opens a content scope beneath it. `expanded` is the app's state (on_change flips it); the content fades up when it appears:

```odin
if baldr.expander(ui, "Advanced", s.more, on_change = proc(e: baldr.Ev(bool)) -> Msg { return More{e.value} }) {
	defer baldr.end(ui)
	…
}
```

Accessibility: the header is a Button named `title` with Expanded / Collapsed; Enter / Space flip it.

### `expander`

```odin
expander :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), title: string, expanded: bool, on_change: Handler(Msg, bool) = nil,
	tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

## widget_feedback.odin

Feedback: progress (a determinate bar, or an indeterminate sweep), spinner (an activity ring of dots) and badge (a small labelled pill). The bars and the spinner keep moving on their own while indeterminate; under reduce-motion they hold still.

### `Tone`

```odin
Tone :: enum u8 { Neutral, Primary, Success, Warning, Danger }
```

Tone colours a badge and other status chrome.

### `progress`

```odin
progress :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), value: f32, label: string = "", width: f32 = 0, flex: f32 = 0, indeterminate := false,
	key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

progress shows `value` in 0..1 (clamped; the fill eases to it) or, with `indeterminate`, a sweeping band. Accessibility: Progress named `label` with the value, or Busy when indeterminate.

### `spinner`

```odin
spinner :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), size: f32 = 20, label: string = "Loading", key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

spinner is an activity ring of eight dots with a bright one going round. Accessibility: Status named `label`, Busy, announced politely.

### `badge`

```odin
badge :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), label: string, tone: Tone = .Neutral, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

badge is a small pill with a word or a number in it: counts, states, tags. Accessibility: plain text.

### `tone_colors`

```odin
tone_colors :: proc(th: ^Theme, tone: Tone) -> (bg, fg: Color)
```

tone_colors: a tinted background and a readable foreground for a tone.

## widget_toast.odin

toast: a brief message that slides up at the bottom of the window and goes away by itself. The app decides which toasts exist (a list in its state); each one sends `on_close` when its time is up, when its × is pressed, or when its `action` is taken (after `on_action`). The pointer resting on a toast keeps it. Several stack upwards. Accessibility: a Status announced politely (assertively for the Danger tone) with a "Dismiss" button.

```odin
for tst in s.toasts do baldr.toast(ui, tst.text, Msg(Toast_Done{tst.id}), action = "Undo", on_action = Msg(Undo{}), key = tst.id)
```

### `toast`

```odin
toast :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), text: string, on_close: Msg, action: string = "", on_action: Maybe(Msg) = nil,
	duration_ms: f32 = 5000, tone: Tone = .Neutral, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

## widget_breadcrumbs.odin

breadcrumbs: where you are, as a trail of links — Home › Pictures › Wallpaper. Every part but the last is a button that sends on_change with its index; the last is the current place. When the trail does not fit the width it is given, the leading parts fold into a "…" link (to the nearest folded one) and as many of the nearest parts as fit stay readable, the parent and the current place always; at phone width a long trail folds to "… › parent › current". In a row, give it `flex` so it knows the room it has. Accessibility: a Group named `label` of Buttons; the current part is Text with Selected.

```odin
baldr.breadcrumbs(ui, parts, proc(e: baldr.Ev(int)) -> Msg { return Go_To{e.value} })
```

### `breadcrumbs`

```odin
breadcrumbs :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), parts: []string, on_change: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, label: string = "Location",
	on_rest: Handler(Msg, Drag_Rest) = nil, flex: f32 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `crumbs_first_shown`

```odin
crumbs_first_shown :: proc(t: ^Tree, parts: []string, room: f32, compact: bool) -> int
```

crumbs_first_shown is the first part left readable: 0 when the trail fits `room` (or no room is known yet), else the nearest parts that fit after a "…", never fewer than the parent and the current place. At phone width a trail of more than three parts keeps only those two.

## widget_tabs.odin

Tabs: a tab bar whose selected indicator glides between tabs, and keep-alive panels that stay in the tree (parked, hidden) while another tab is showing, so scroll positions, carets, and expanded rows survive a switch at zero rebuild cost.

```odin
   baldr.tab_bar(ui, int(s.tab), {"Files", "Search", "Settings"}, on_change = Msg.Tab_Changed)
   if baldr.tab_panel(ui, s.tab == .Files,    key = 1) { defer baldr.end(ui); files(ui, s) }
   if baldr.tab_panel(ui, s.tab == .Search,   key = 2) { defer baldr.end(ui); search(ui, s) }
```

### `tab_bar`

```odin
tab_bar :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), selected: int, labels: []string, on_change: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	tones: []Tone = nil,
	key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

tab_bar draws one button per label; clicking sends the tab's index through `on_change`.  `tones` is a tone per tab, for a tab that asks to be noticed (requests waiting, a failed sync). A tab with a tone other than `.Neutral` is drawn in that tone while it is not selected, and as an ordinary selected tab once it is, when the colour would only be noise. Shorter than `labels`, or nil, is `.Neutral` throughout.  Accessible as a Tab_List of Tabs with the selected state; a tab asking for attention is also described as doing so, since colour alone is not something everyone can see.

### `tab_bars_reveal_selected`

```odin
tab_bars_reveal_selected :: proc(t: ^Tree) -> (moved: bool)
```

tab_bars_reveal_selected scrolls every tab bar so its selected tab is in view, instantly — called after a resize, when a bar that fit before may now clip the current tab. Returns true if any bar moved.

### `tab_panel`

```odin
tab_panel :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), active: bool, key: u64, flex: f32 = 1, gap: Space = Space_Token.none,
	padding: Space = Space_Token.none, scroll: bool = false, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

tab_panel opens the content scope for one tab when `active`; when not, its node is parked hidden (Keep) so its state survives. Returns whether the scope is open — close it with end only then. `key` must be unique among sibling panels (it identifies the parked node). `scroll` makes the panel itself a vertical scroll region (wheel, keys, overlay bar at the panel's edge, focus revealed) for pages taller than the window; its offset survives switching away. Leave it off for a page whose content fills the panel (a list or table with `flex = 1`), which scrolls on its own.

## widget_list.odin

list is a virtualised vertical list: only the rows that intersect the viewport (plus a small overscan) are built each view; the rest is represented by two spacers, so a hundred thousand rows cost what a screen of them does. Rows have one fixed `row_height` (plus `gap`); build them yourself between list and end, keyed by index:

```odin
first, last := baldr.list(ui, len(s.rows), row_height = 56, label = "Inbox"); {
	defer baldr.end(ui)
	for i in first ..< last {
		baldr.card(ui, height = 56, key = u64(i + 1)); { defer baldr.end(ui); … }
	}
}
```

**Rows of different heights**: pass `estimated_height` instead of `row_height` and each row sizes to its content.

```odin
first, last := baldr.list(ui, len(s.msgs), estimated_height = 72, label = "Chat")
```

A row is measured once it has been laid out and its height kept by its key, so rows loaded above do not renumber what is known. Rows never built stand at the estimate, so it should be near the common case: a bad one costs a visible settle, not correctness. The window is found by a binary search over the measured tops.

**A list that knows its rows' heights** gives `height_of` instead (pages at the current zoom, a table of two row kinds): nothing is estimated or measured, the offsets are exact from the first build, and each row is made the height given. Use it when every row changes height together, which a list of estimates only catches up with as it measures again.

```odin
first, last := baldr.list(ui, len(s.pages), height_of = page_height, key_user = s)
```

**Keeping the reader's place.** Give `key_of` so a row's identity does not move when the app prepends; then `follow_end` rides the newest row (and lets go the moment the reader scrolls up), and `on_at` reports a `List_At` whenever the resting place changes. `key_of` is asked only between this call and its `end`, so `key_user` may point at the build's temp memory; the key for an index must not change between two builds passed the same rows, or the heights follow the wrong rows.

**Moving the view** is a command from `update` (reveal_cmd.odin), never a parameter: scrolling to a row happens once, and a view is rebuilt constantly. `reveal_key` sends it to a row (`table` and `icon_view` take `reveal_index`); revealing the last row pins to the end and follows again, so `at_end` is reported at once. `hold` and `hold_at`, returned with the zoom that needs them, keep a point still across it: applied in the build that changed the scale, before the range is chosen, so no frame is built from the old offsets. With `height_of` it lands exactly; with estimates it is out by their error above the row, so ask again on the next build.

```odin
baldr.hold_at(Msg, PAGES, at = {0, y}, axes = {.Y})  // keep what is there
baldr.hold(Msg, PAGES, row, point, at)               // …or a named point
```

Prefer `hold_at`: it names a spot of the viewport, resolved from the offset the list is really on, where an app's own note can be a scroll behind (the list does not rebuild for a scroll that keeps the same rows on screen), and it keeps a fraction of the row, so there is no scale to apply. `hold`'s `point` is px into the row, which with `content_width` is that whole width, measured from the content's left edge and not inside the list's padding; `axes = {.Y}` spares that arithmetic.

**`gutter`** keeps every row clear of the overlay scrollbar, always reserved, not only while the content overflows; pass it when rows put something at their right edge, which the bar would otherwise cover. `table` and `icon_view` take it off their column widths, so headings stay above their cells.

**`content_width`** makes it scroll sideways too: rows are laid out that wide, a naturally wider row keeps its width and the others widen to match, and a bar is drawn along the bottom. It is the app's number because only built rows are measured, and the widest of those would move the extent as it scrolls. The wheel's x, Shift+wheel, a touchpad, `scroll_by` / `scroll_to` on `list_node(ui)` and Shift+Page/Home/End pan it; virtualisation stays by row, down.

Scrolling that changes the visible range re-runs the view (Ui.needs_rebuild). Rows scrolled out of range are destroyed, so keep row state in the app, not in the widget. Accessibility: role List named by `label`; Page Up/Down, Home and End scroll it (with Shift, sideways where it scrolls sideways), and keyboard focus scrolls rows into view.

### `List_At`

```odin
List_At :: struct {
	first_key:  u64,
	at_end:     bool, // riding the newest row
	near_start: bool, // within a viewport of the oldest: time to fetch more
}
```

List_At is where a list is resting, for an app that must act on it: a chat fetches older history as the reader nears the top, and shows a "jump to newest" button when they are not at the bottom.  `first_key` is the key of the row the top of the viewport is on, so the app can remember the place across a restart.

### `Row_Key`

```odin
Row_Key :: proc(user: rawptr, index: int) -> u64
```

Row_Key is a row's stable identity: given an index, the key the app builds that row with. The default, `u64(i + 1)`, is renumbered by a prepend and the heights would follow the wrong rows, so give one for any list whose rows can be prepended or removed (one that only appends needs none). It is called only during the build, for an index within that build's `count`, so `user` may point at the frame's temp memory. It must be a pure function of the app's rows: the list reuses the last build's answers between builds, so a key that changes its mind (a random id, a key folded in from selection or hover) misplaces the heights.

### `Row_Height`

```odin
Row_Height :: proc(user: rawptr, index: int) -> f32
```

Row_Height is a row's height for a list that knows it rather than measuring it (a page at the current zoom, a table of 32 and 64 px rows). Passed as `height_of`, it makes each row exactly this tall, so the offsets are exact on the first build and a reveal or hold lands at once. Like `Row_Key` it is asked only during the build, with `key_user`, so temp memory is fine; it is asked for every row when the offsets are redone and for the rows on screen every build, so it must be cheap arithmetic. Rows changing height together need nothing else said: the visible rows disagreeing with the offsets is how the list notices, and a row changed off screen is noticed when it comes back into range.

### `list_node`

```odin
list_node :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg)) -> Node
```

list_node is the node of the `list` whose scope is open: what `scroll_by`, `scroll_to` and `scroll_offset` take, since `list` returns the row range rather than the node. A view that scrolls its own list asks for it between `list` and its `end`:  first, last := baldr.list(ui, len(s.blocks), estimated_height = 400) s.body = baldr.list_node(ui)              // kept for the next Space for i in first ..< last do row(ui, i, s) baldr.end(ui)  NODE_NONE when the open scope is not a list's, as after `end`. The node lives as long as the list does, so read it in the build that uses it rather than keeping it for ever.

### `list`

```odin
list :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), count: int, row_height: f32 = 0, estimated_height: f32 = 0,
	height_of: Row_Height = nil,
	key_of: Row_Key = nil, key_user: rawptr = nil, follow_end := false,
	on_at: Handler(Msg, List_At) = nil,
	gap: Space = Space_Token.none, padding: Space = Space_Token.none,
	flex: f32 = 1, height: f32 = 0, label: string = "", tail_pad: f32 = 0, on_range: Handler(Msg, [2]int) = nil,
	gutter := false, content_width: f32 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> (first, last: int)
```

## widget_table.odin

table is a grid with a sticky header and virtual rows: only the visible rows exist as nodes (it is a `list` underneath). Columns are fixed (`width`) or flexible (`flex`, the default); the header's dividers show as hairlines, the pointer becomes a resize cursor over them and a guide runs down the table while one is dragged; a header cell dragged sideways moves its column to another slot (a drop indicator shows where). A click on a sortable header asks the app to sort (`on_sort`; the app reorders its data). With a header cell focused, Shift+Left/Right resizes and Ctrl+Left/Right reorders, so nothing needs the pointer.

Rows are selected by click, Ctrl-click, Shift-click, and the keyboard cursor (Up/Down, Shift to extend, Space to toggle, Ctrl+A, Home/End, Page Up/Down) — the app-owned `Selection` is edited in place and `on_select` carries the cursor row; Enter or a double-click sends `on_activate`. Column order and dragged widths are the table's own state (like a scroll offset); columns are declared and cells built in the declared order regardless. Accessibility: Table named by `label`, Column_Header cells (Selected = sorted), Row (Selected) named by its first cell, Cell nodes; the table is the focusable widget.

```odin
first, last := baldr.table(ui, COLS, len(s.files), &s.sel, on_select = …, on_activate = …, sort = s.sort, on_sort = …, label = "Files"); {
	defer baldr.end(ui)
	for i in first ..< last {
		baldr.table_row(ui, i); {
			defer baldr.end(ui)
			baldr.cell_text(ui, s.files[i].name)
			baldr.cell_text(ui, size_str(s.files[i].size))
		}
	}
}
```

### `Column`

```odin
Column :: struct {
	title:     string,
	width:     f32,  // fixed logical px; 0 = flexible
	flex:      f32,  // share of the free width for a flexible column (0 = 1)
	min_width: f32,  // flexible columns never go narrower (0 = 60)
	numeric:   bool, // right-aligned cells
	sortable:  bool,
}
```

Column describes one column of a `table`: its heading, how wide it is (`width` for a fixed size, otherwise `flex` shares the free width and `min_width` bounds it), whether its cells are right-aligned numbers, and whether its header sorts. Pass a slice of these to `table`; the app owns the slice and the strings in it, and they must outlive the view.

### `Sort`

```odin
Sort :: struct {
	column:     int,
	descending: bool,
}
```

Sort is the app's current sort; `column` -1 = none. Shown in the header.

### `Row_Ref`

```odin
Row_Ref :: struct {
	table: Node,
	index: int,
}
```

Row_Ref names one row of one table — the table's node and the row index — as a table's handlers report it back.

### `Col_Ref`

```odin
Col_Ref :: struct {
	table: Node,
	col:   int, // display slot
}
```

Col_Ref names one column of one table, by the slot it is displayed in rather than by its place in the `Column` slice, which a column move changes.

### `Col_Resize`

```odin
Col_Resize :: struct {
	table:   Node,
	col:     int, // display slot
	start_x: f32,
	start_w: f32,
}
```

Col_Resize is a drag on a table's column divider: where it started and how wide the column was then.

### `Col_Move`

```odin
Col_Move :: struct {
	table:    Node,
	slot:     int,
	start_x:  f32,
	dragging: bool,
}
```

Col_Move is a drag of a table's column header to another slot, and whether it has travelled far enough to count as a move rather than a click.

### `table`

```odin
table :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), columns: []Column, count: int, selection: ^Selection = nil,
	on_select: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, on_click: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	on_activate: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, on_context: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	sort: Sort = {column = -1}, on_sort: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	drag: string = "", on_drop: Handler(Msg, Drop) = nil, accepts: string = "", drag_files: Files_Of = nil, drag_user: rawptr = nil, drag_files_out: Files_Out = nil,
	on_rest: Handler(Msg, Drag_Rest) = nil,
	on_drag_out: Handler(Msg, Drop) = nil, on_range: Handler(Msg, [2]int) = nil,
	on_zoom: Handler(Msg, f32) = nil, on_edit: Handler(Msg, Cell_Ref) = nil,
	label_of: Label_Of = nil, label_user: rawptr = nil,
	row_height: f32 = 0, rows: int = 0, flex: f32 = 1, height: f32 = 0, label: string = "",
	gutter := false, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> (first, last: int)
```

table builds the grid and opens the scope its rows go in; it returns the `first, last` rows to build. `rows` sizes it to that many rows plus its header instead of filling the space given (an explicit `height` wins, and a sized table does not flex), so an app never estimates a row height from its fonts. `row_height` 0 takes the theme's (`table_look`); an app's own sets the header's height too. `on_select` follows what is selected, every arrow key included, so a row that opens on one click wants `on_click`: a plain primary click (no Ctrl or Shift, not the second of a double), as on `tree_item`. `on_activate` is a double-click or Enter. An Enter or Space the app bound with `shortcut` is the app's, and with no `on_activate` Enter goes on to the window.

### `table_row`

```odin
table_row :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), index: int, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

table_row opens the row for data index `index` (keyed by it). Build one cell per column inside, in declared order, then end.

### `cell`

```odin
cell :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

cell opens the next cell of the row being built (the next declared column's slot). Close with end. Use cell_text for the common case.

### `cell_text`

```odin
cell_text :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), s: string, muted := false, icon: Icon = .None, tabular := false, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

cell_text is a cell holding one line of text (clipped, never wrapped); the first cell's text names the row for assistive technology.

### `table_key`

```odin
table_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

table_key handles keys while a table (or one of its header cells) has focus. Returns true if consumed.

## widget_icon_view.odin

icon_view is the grid a file manager shows: tiles of an icon over a label, laid out in as many columns as fit, virtualised by row (only the visible rows exist as nodes). It shares `Selection` with table: click, Ctrl-click and Shift-click, a rubber band dragged over empty space (Ctrl adds to the selection), arrows that move the cursor across the grid, Shift to extend, Space, Ctrl+A, Home/End; `on_select` carries the cursor, Enter or a double-click sends `on_activate`. Accessibility: List named by `label`, List_Item tiles (Selected) named by their labels; the view is the focusable widget.

```odin
first, last := baldr.icon_view(ui, len(s.files), &s.sel, on_select = …, on_activate = …, label = "Files"); {
	defer baldr.end(ui)
	for i in first ..< last do baldr.icon_item(ui, i, s.files[i].icon, s.files[i].name)
}
```

With `place_of` it is a placed grid instead of a flow — a desktop, where an icon stays where it was put. `place_of` gives each item its cell (`{-1, -1}`: the view picks a free one, flowing down the columns), a drag inside the view reports where it landed through `on_place`, and the app writes that down and hands it back; the view keeps no placement of its own, just as it keeps no selection. In that mode the arrows move by geometry — the nearest item up, down, left or right — and the band selects whoever is in the cells it covers.

### `Icon_View_Variant`

```odin
Icon_View_Variant :: enum u8 { Icons, Pictures }
```

Icon_View_Variant is how an icon view's tiles look: an icon or thumbnail with a label centred under it (files, apps), or a picture filling a square with a title and a muted subtitle under it, and an action over it on hover (albums, books).

### `PICTURE_TILE`

```odin
PICTURE_TILE :: [2]f32{168, 214}
```

PICTURE_TILE is a picture tile's default size: a cover and two lines.

### `icon_view`

```odin
icon_view :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), count: int, selection: ^Selection = nil,
	on_select: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, on_click: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	on_activate: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, on_context: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	drag: string = "", on_drop: Handler(Msg, Drop) = nil, accepts: string = "", drag_files: Files_Of = nil, drag_user: rawptr = nil, drag_files_out: Files_Out = nil,
	on_rest: Handler(Msg, Drag_Rest) = nil,
	on_drag_out: Handler(Msg, Drop) = nil, on_range: Handler(Msg, [2]int) = nil,
	on_zoom: Handler(Msg, f32) = nil, on_edit: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	label_of: Label_Of = nil, label_user: rawptr = nil, animate := false, label_over: Label_Over = .Surface,
	place_of: Place_Of = nil, place_user: rawptr = nil, on_place: Handler(Msg, Place_Drop) = nil,
	tile: [2]f32 = {}, gap: Space = nil, padding: Space = nil,
	flex: f32 = 1, height: f32 = 0, label: string = "", variant: Icon_View_Variant = .Icons,
	gutter := false, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> (first, last: int)
```

icon_view opens the grid and returns the items to build with icon_item; end(ui) closes it. `on_select` follows what is selected, every arrow key included; `on_click` is the gesture — a plain primary click on an item, no Ctrl or Shift and not the second click of a double — so "one click opens" belongs there, as on `tree_item`; `on_activate` is a double-click or Enter. An Enter or Space the app bound with `shortcut` is the app's, and with no `on_activate` Enter goes on to the window. `tile`, `gap` and `padding` left unset take the theme's (`icon_view_look`).

### `Label_Over`

```odin
Label_Over :: enum u8 { Surface, Anything }
```

Label_Over says what an icon view's labels are drawn over, because a label can only pick a colour that reads if it knows. `.Surface` is the app's own background, where the theme's colours are right. `.Anything` is a wallpaper, a photograph, a video — something the app did not choose — and there the labels and icons carry a halo in the opposite tone, the one thing that works where one label is on sky and the next on a hedge.

### `Place_Of`

```odin
Place_Of :: #type proc(user: rawptr, index: int) -> [2]int
```

Place_Of says which cell an item belongs in — column and row, counted from the view's top-left. `{-1, -1}` means the app has not placed this one and the view should put it somewhere free.

### `Place_Drop`

```odin
Place_Drop :: struct {
	items: []int,    // the dragged items (temp memory: valid while the message is handled)
	cells: [][2]int, // where each of `items` lands: the shape kept, a taken cell moved to the nearest free one
	cell:  [2]int,   // the cell the pointer was over, untouched — for an app that would rather work it out itself
	item:  int,      // the dragged item that was under the pointer: the one that lands on `cell`
}
```

Place_Drop is a drag landing in a placed view: which items moved, and the cell the pointer was over when they were let go. The app writes it down and gives it back through `place_of`: the view keeps no placement of its own.

### `icon_item`

```odin
icon_item :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), index: int, which: Icon, label: string, picture: ^Image = nil,
	on_submit: Handler(Msg, string) = nil, on_cancel: Maybe(Msg) = nil, subtitle: string = "",
	action: Maybe(Msg) = nil, action_icon: Icon = .Play, action_label: string = "", loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

icon_item adds tile `index` (an icon over a label) to the view being built; rows open and close by themselves. With `on_submit` the label is an editor instead (after the view's `on_edit` asked for it): it takes focus with the name selected up to its extension, Enter or leaving commits through `on_submit`, Escape sends `on_cancel`. In a `.Pictures` view the tile is `picture` filling a square, cropped (or `which` on a quiet square), with `label` as its title and `subtitle` muted under it, a line each ended with "…", the subtitle heard after the name; `action` (named by `action_label`, drawn as `action_icon`) is a button over the picture's corner, shown on hover or for the keyboard's tile, and Tab from the view reaches the current tile's.

### `LABEL_HALO_RADIUS`

```odin
LABEL_HALO_RADIUS :: f32(2)
```

The halo a label wears over a wallpaper: tight, and laid down several times. A glow is a Gaussian of sigma = radius / 2, and one pass over an 11 px glyph is too thin to outline it: over a pale background one pass reaches about 2:1, four reach 9.5:1. The cost is four small quads per glyph, only in a view that asked to be legible over something unknown.

### `icon_view_key`

```odin
icon_view_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

## widget_tree.odin

tree_view + tree_item: a hierarchy with expand / collapse — a file manager's sidebar, a project explorer, settings sections. Expansion and selection are the app's state, echoed back through on_change and on_click, so a directory can be loaded the first time it is opened. A branch's children scope is open only while it is expanded:

```odin
baldr.tree_view(ui, label = "Project"); {
	defer baldr.end(ui)
	if baldr.tree_item(ui, "src", Msg(Pick{1}), expanded = s.open[1], icon = .Folder, key = 1,
		on_change = proc(e: baldr.Ev(bool)) -> Msg { return Open{e.tag, e.value} }, tag = 1) {
		defer baldr.end(ui)
		baldr.tree_item(ui, "main.odin", Msg(Pick{2}), icon = .File_Code, key = 2)
	}
}
```

An item is a branch when it has an on_change (the chevron toggles it), a leaf otherwise. `drag` makes the row a drag source of that kind (tag = the item's tag); `on_drop` makes it a target — On a branch (drop into the folder), Before / After a leaf (reorder) — see dnd.odin. Keyboard, with the tree view focused (the cursor row shows the ring, one Tab stop for the whole tree): Up / Down move and select, Right expands or steps into the first child, Left collapses or steps to the parent, Home / End, Enter activates (on_activate, else on_click) unless the app bound that Enter with `shortcut`, Space selects; a double-click activates. Accessibility: Tree of Tree_Items with Expanded / Collapsed and Selected states. Not virtualised — every visible item is a node — so put a large tree inside scroll.

### `Tree_Row`

```odin
Tree_Row :: struct {
	tree:       Node, // the tree view it belongs to
	chevron:    Node, // the expander, NONE for a leaf
	container:  Node, // the children scope while expanded, else NONE
	depth:      int,
	expandable: bool,
	expanded:   bool,
}
```

Tree_Row is what the tree knows about one item's row (Tree side table, so paint and keys can read it without the message type).

### `tree_view`

```odin
tree_view :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), label: string = "", flex: f32 = 0, height: f32 = 0, indent: Maybe(f32) = nil,
	padding: Space = Space_Token.none, on_edit: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

tree_view opens a tree. Give it a size (`height`, or `flex` in a column) or let it grow with its items inside a scroll. Close with end. `indent` is px per level; nil takes the theme's (`tree_row.indent`).

### `tree_item`

```odin
tree_item :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), label: string, on_click: Msg, expanded := false, on_change: Handler(Msg, bool) = nil,
	selected := false, icon: Icon = .None, on_activate: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, disabled := false,
	drag: string = "", on_drop: Handler(Msg, Drop) = nil, accepts: string = "", on_rest: Handler(Msg, Drag_Rest) = nil,
	on_submit: Handler(Msg, string) = nil, on_cancel: Maybe(Msg) = nil,
	on_context: Handler(Msg, int) = nil, on_middle_click: Maybe(Msg) = nil, drop_positions: Drop_Positions = {},
	tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

tree_item adds one row to the open tree (or branch). Returns whether its children scope is open — close it with end only then. Items without a `key` are identified by their label.

### `tree_rows_in_order`

```odin
tree_rows_in_order :: proc(t: ^Tree, tree: Node, allocator := context.temp_allocator) -> []Node
```

tree_rows_in_order lists a tree's visible rows top to bottom, in temp memory by default.

### `tree_key`

```odin
tree_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

tree_key moves the focused tree's cursor (selection follows it), expands and collapses with Left / Right, activates with Enter.

## widget_split.odin

split + pane: two panes side by side (or stacked) with a draggable divider — sidebar and content, editor and preview, list and detail. The sized pane's extent is the app's state, echoed through on_change while the divider is dragged or nudged, so it can be remembered:

```odin
baldr.split(ui, s.sidebar_w, on_change = proc(e: baldr.Ev(f32)) -> Msg { return Sidebar{e.value} }, min = 160, max = 480); {
	defer baldr.end(ui)
	baldr.pane(ui, padding = .sm); { defer baldr.end(ui); sidebar(s, ui) }
	baldr.pane(ui, padding = .lg); { defer baldr.end(ui); content(s, ui) }
}
```

`gap` is a gutter either side of the divider, so the panes' content can sit flush with the split's top and bottom and still breathe sideways. The divider is a hairline with an 8 px grab zone (24 px on touch) that shows the resize cursor and an accent bar on hover; it is a Tab stop (role Separator, value = size): Left / Right (Up / Down when stacked) move it by 8 px, Shift by 32, Home / End to the limits. `min` bounds both panes; `max` the sized one.

Phones show one pane at a time. `collapse = .Compact` (or `.Medium`) makes the split do that below the given size class: the first pane fills the window until `detail` is true, then the second pane fronts it with a Back button (`back_label`) that sends `on_close`; Escape sends it too. The app keeps `detail` in its state (picked something and not gone back), so resizing keeps whatever the split was showing:

```odin
baldr.split(ui, s.sidebar_w, on_change = …, collapse = .Compact,
	detail = s.sel >= 0 && !s.dismissed, on_close = Msg(Back{}), back_label = "Project")
```

### `Split_Sized`

```odin
Split_Sized :: enum u8 { First, Second }
```

Split_Sized says which pane `size` applies to; the other pane flexes.

### `Collapse`

```odin
Collapse :: enum u8 { Never, Compact, Medium }
```

Collapse: at and below which size class the split shows one pane at a time.

### `split`

```odin
split :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), size: f32, on_change: Handler(Msg, f32) = nil, axis: Axis = .Row,
	min: f32 = SPLIT_MIN_DEFAULT, max: f32 = 0, sized: Split_Sized = .First,
	collapse: Collapse = .Never, detail := false, on_close: Maybe(Msg) = nil, back_label: string = "Back",
	gap: Space = Space_Token.none, flex: f32 = 1, width: f32 = 0, height: f32 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

split opens the container: exactly two `pane` scopes follow. Close with end.

### `pane`

```odin
pane :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), gap: Space = Space_Token.none, padding: Space = Space_Token.none, align: Align = .Stretch,
	bg: Color = {}, glass := false, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

pane opens one side of the open split: the first call is the first pane. `glass` gives it the theme's sidebar material — thinner than a window's own tint, over what the compositor blurs behind the window — for a sidebar beside opaque content; the window itself declares `clear` with no tint, so the sidebar's glass is the only tint there. Where glass isn't live (Solid, a compositor that can't blur, an opaque window) the pane is the sidebar's colour, opaque, unless `bg` gives one, so a see-through window never shows the desktop through its sidebar. Close with end.

### `split_escape`

```odin
split_escape :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg)) -> bool
```

split_escape: Escape leaves a collapsed split's detail pane (sends on_close), after any open overlay has had its turn.

### `split_key`

```odin
split_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

split_key nudges a focused divider.

## widget_grid.odin

Shared behaviour of the two selection grids, table (one column of rows) and icon view (tiles): item clicks with Ctrl / Shift and double-click activation, the rubber band dragged over empty space, and context requests (right-click, the Menu key, Shift+F10) on an item or on the background. A file manager needs all of these in both views.

### `Grid_Sel`

```odin
Grid_Sel :: struct {
	marquee: bool,
	m_start: [2]f32, // content coordinates: inside the list, scrolled
	m_cur:   [2]f32,
	m_add:   bool,   // Ctrl held: keep the selection that was there
	base:    map[int]struct{},
}
```

Grid_Sel is the rubber-band state kept by a table or icon view.

### `Grid_Geom`

```odin
Grid_Geom :: struct {
	list:  Node,
	cols:  int,
	tile:  [2]f32,
	gap:   f32,
	count: int,
	sel:   ^Selection,
	g:     ^Grid_Sel,
	placed: ^Icon_View_State, // a placed icon view: cells hold whatever the app put there, not index order
}
```

Grid_Geom describes a grid for arithmetic selection: `cols` tiles of `tile` size with `gap` between them, `count` items in all.

### `Band_Pending`

```odin
Band_Pending :: struct {
	view:  Node,
	start: [2]f32,
}
```

Band_Pending is a press on the blank part of a table row (past a cell's text): a click there selects the row as usual, a drag past the slop turns it into a rubber band from the press point.

## widget_menu_bar.odin

menu_bar is the classic File / Edit / View strip. It owns which menu is open (like select owns its list): a click on a title opens it, hovering another title while one is open switches, a press elsewhere or Escape closes; keyboard: F10 (or Tab) reaches the titles, Left / Right move between them (switching an open menu too), Down / Enter / Space open, and inside a menu Up / Down walk the items, Left / Right jump to the neighbouring menu, Escape closes and returns focus to the title. Accessibility: Menu_Bar of Menu_Item titles (with a popup, Expanded / Collapsed) whose dropdowns are Menus of Menu_Items. Mnemonics (Alt+F) come later.

```odin
baldr.menu_bar(ui); {
	defer baldr.end(ui)
	if baldr.menu_bar_menu(ui, "File") { defer baldr.end(ui)
		baldr.menu_item(ui, "New", Msg(New{}), shortcut = "Ctrl+N", icon = .File_Plus)
		baldr.separator(ui)
		baldr.menu_item(ui, "Quit", Msg(Quit{}), shortcut = "Ctrl+Q")
	}
	if baldr.menu_bar_menu(ui, "Edit") { defer baldr.end(ui); … }
}
```

### `menu_bar`

```odin
menu_bar :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

### `menu_bar_menu`

```odin
menu_bar_menu :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), title: string, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

menu_bar_menu adds a title to the bar being built and, while its menu is open, opens the dropdown scope for its items. Returns whether the scope is open; close it with end.

### `menu_bar_key`

```odin
menu_bar_key :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: Key, mods: Mods) -> bool
```

menu_bar_key handles keys for a focused title or a focused item of an open dropdown. Returns true if consumed.

## overlay.odin

Overlays: popover, select, dialog. They are built into the overlay root (Tree.overlay), a stack that covers the window and is painted last, so any view proc can open one from anywhere in the tree. Positions come from the anchor's rect in the retained tree, so no second layout pass is needed. Dismissal — a press outside, or Escape — either queues the app's message (popover, dialog) or clears the widget's own open state (select).

### `Placement`

```odin
Placement :: enum u8 { Below, Above, Right, Left, Over }
```

Placement: which side of its anchor an overlay sits on, or `.Over`: on the anchor itself, aligned across it by `align` and moved by `nudge`, covering it and its neighbours (an icon tile's rename editor, which grows past its tile).

### `Dismiss_Rule`

```odin
Dismiss_Rule :: struct($Msg: typeid) {
	fired:   bool, // already dismissed this build; kept until the sweep for `restore`
	has_msg: bool,
	msg:     Msg,
	owner:   Node, // select trigger whose `open` flag is cleared
	anchor:  Node,
	inside:  Node,
	restore: Node, // where focus goes when the overlay closes with focus inside it
	// …and where it goes when Escape closed it, when that differs: a menu
	// bar's dropdown returns focus to its title on Escape so the bar stays
	// navigable, but to the previous holder when an item is chosen or the
	// menu is clicked away.
	restore_key: Node,
	by_key:      bool, // Escape closed it, so `restore_key` decides
	// A press on the scrim does not dismiss this one: a form dialog's
	// Cancel, Escape and ✕ still do, but a stray click must not discard
	// what was typed.
	keep_on_outside: bool,
}
```

Dismiss_Rule: how an open overlay closes. `inside` is the rect a press must land in to NOT dismiss (the popover itself, or a dialog's card); presses on `anchor` are left to the anchor (it toggles itself).

### `POPOVER_ENTER_MS`

```odin
POPOVER_ENTER_MS :: f32(140)
```

How long a popover takes to arrive and to go, in ms. Not theme motion tokens: `fast` is for a state layer and `base` for something the eye follows, and a menu should be there almost at once and gone the moment it is dismissed. motion.reduce makes both instant.

### `Overlay_Rule`

```odin
Overlay_Rule :: struct {
	anchor:    Node,
	placement: Placement,
	gap:       f32,
	cover:     bool,
	fixed:     bool,   // placed at `at` (a context menu at the pointer), kept inside the viewport
	at:        [2]f32,
	// How much room above `at` a flip must leave as well (0: `at` is a
	// point, as a context menu's pointer is). A card hanging off a line (a
	// completion list under the caret) that flips above must clear that
	// line, or it covers the text being typed.
	at_avoid:  f32,
	// Kept inside this node's width as well as the window's, so a card
	// centred on a tile in a view's first column does not spill over a
	// sidebar beside it. Height is free: a rename on the last row may grow
	// past the view, stopped only by the window. NONE: the window alone.
	within:    Node,
	nudge:     [2]f32, // added after placement (a submenu lines its first item up with its row)
	align:     Align,  // along the anchor: .Start (its start with the anchor's), .Center, .End; .Stretch is .Start
	edge:      Overlay_Edge, // hugs the viewport's bottom or top, centred (toasts); `stack` counts earlier ones
	stack:     int,
	edge_margin: f32, // how far an edge overlay keeps from that edge
	stack_gap:   f32, // between edge overlays stacked on one another
}
```

Overlay_Rule places an overlay-root child at layout time, when its size and the anchor's rect are current: anchored next to `anchor`, or covering the viewport (a dialog's scrim).

### `Overlay_Edge`

```odin
Overlay_Edge :: enum u8 { None, Bottom, Top }
```

Overlay_Edge is which screen edge an overlay has been pushed against, so it can be kept a margin clear of it.

### `popover`

```odin
popover :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), anchor: Node, open: bool, dismiss: Msg, placement: Placement = .Below,
	min_width: f32 = 0, glass := true, align: Align = .Start, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

popover opens a floating surface anchored to `anchor` while `open` is true. A press outside it (and outside the anchor) or Escape queues `dismiss`; the app then sets its open flag to false. Close the scope with end. Returns whether the scope is open. `align` is where it sits along the anchor: `.Start` (its start with the anchor's, the default), `.Center` (its middle over the anchor's — a dock's menu over its button) or `.End`; it slides back onto the screen as any placement does.

### `dismiss_outside`

```odin
dismiss_outside :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), pos: [2]f32) -> (any: bool)
```

dismiss_outside fires every open overlay's rule whose `inside` rect (and anchor) does not contain `pos`. Returns true if any closed.

### `dismiss_all`

```odin
dismiss_all :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg)) -> (any: bool)
```

dismiss_all closes every open overlay and hides the tooltip. Escape uses dismiss_escape, which closes the innermost first.

### `menu_nav`

```odin
menu_nav :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), forward: bool) -> bool
```

menu_nav moves the highlight to the next/previous focusable inside the overlay child that contains the focus (a select's list, a menu), from the item the keys act on (menu_current). When nothing is highlighted yet, a menu opened with the pointer, Down goes to the first item and Up to the last. False if focus is not inside an overlay.

### `SELECT_FIT_MAX`

```odin
SELECT_FIT_MAX :: f32(420)
```

SELECT_FIT_MAX is the widest a select sizes itself to for its options (`width = 0`); a longer option is cut, as one too long for a set width is.

### `select`

```odin
select :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), selected: int, options: []string, on_change: Handler(Msg, int) = nil,
	placeholder: string = "Select…", width: f32 = 200, disabled := false, label: string = "",
	tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

select is a drop-down: a trigger showing the selected option, and a popup list while open. `width = 0` sizes it to its widest option at the text size in force (the desktop's included), so the box does not change as options are picked. Open state is the widget's own (click, Enter, Space toggle it; an outside press or Escape closes it); choosing an option sends its index through `on_change` and closes. An option too long for `width` is cut with "…" on one line. Roles: Combo_Box trigger (with a popup, Expanded while open), Menu with Menu_Items.

### `dialog`

```odin
dialog :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), open: bool, dismiss: Msg, title: string = "", width: f32 = 0,
	dismiss_outside := false,
	key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

dialog opens a modal: a scrim over the window and a centred card that traps keyboard focus. Escape queues `dismiss`. Build the card's content inside and close with end. Returns whether it is open.  A press on the scrim does nothing unless `dismiss_outside` is set, so a stray click cannot discard a form's typed work; Escape, Cancel and ✕ still close it. Set `dismiss_outside = true` for a dialog holding nothing worth keeping (a picture at full size, an about box).  `dismiss` must be the safe answer, never the destructive one: Escape and a scrim press mean "not now". Put anything destructive on a button inside the dialog, and have `dismiss` keep everything and ask again; the toolkit cannot tell a destructive message from a safe one.  `width = 0` takes the theme's (Dialog_Style.width, 420 by default).

### `menu`

```odin
menu :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), anchor: Node, open: bool, dismiss: Msg, placement: Placement = .Below,
	min_width: f32 = 180, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

menu is a popover that behaves as a menu: role Menu, keyboard focus moves onto its first item when it opens (Up/Down walk, Enter activates, Escape closes) and returns to `anchor` when it closes. Fill it with menu_item and separator; an activated item queues its message and the menu's `dismiss`.

### `context_menu`

```odin
context_menu :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), open: bool, dismiss: Msg, min_width: f32 = 180,
	key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

context_menu is a menu that opens where the latest right-click, Menu key or drop happened (`Ui.context_pos`, recorded before `on_context` or `on_drop` fires). Use it with a table's or icon view's `on_context`: the message tells the app which item (or -1 for the background) to build the menu for; or after an Alt-drop (`Drop.action == .Ask`) for Copy here / Move here.  if baldr.context_menu(ui, s.ctx_open, Msg(Ctx_Close{})) { defer baldr.end(ui) if s.ctx_item >= 0 { baldr.menu_item(ui, "Open", …) } else { baldr.menu_item(ui, "New folder", …) } }

### `menu_item`

```odin
menu_item :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), label: string, on_click: Msg, shortcut: string = "", disabled := false,
	icon: Icon = .None, theme_icon := "", checked: Maybe(bool) = nil, radio := false, tag: u64 = 0, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

menu_item is one row of a menu: label left, optional shortcut hint right. Activating it (click, Enter, Space) queues `on_click` and closes the menu. `checked` (a bool, or nil for a plain item) shows a check mark — or a dot when `radio` — in a leading slot and sets the Checked state; give the plain items of such a menu `checked = false` so their labels line up. `icon` or `theme_icon` (an icon theme name: a jump-list action's Icon=, a dbusmenu icon-name) goes before the label.

### `submenu`

```odin
submenu :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), label: string, icon: Icon = .None, theme_icon := "", disabled := false, min_width: f32 = 180,
	key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> bool
```

submenu is a menu row that opens a further menu beside it: when the pointer rests on it, on click, Right, Enter or Space. Inside it, Left or Escape close it and focus returns to the row; the pointer resting on another row of the parent closes it too. Fill it with menu_item / submenu / separator and close with end only when it returns true. An item activated inside closes the whole chain of menus. `icon` or `theme_icon` goes before the label, as on a menu_item. Role Menu_Item (with a popup, Expanded / Collapsed) whose popup is a Menu.  if baldr.submenu(ui, "Sort by", icon = .Arrow_Down_Up) { defer baldr.end(ui) baldr.menu_item(ui, "Name", By_Name{}, checked = s.sort == .Name, radio = true) baldr.menu_item(ui, "Size", By_Size{}, checked = s.sort == .Size, radio = true) }

### `separator`

```odin
separator :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

separator is a hairline between groups: horizontal in a column or menu, vertical in a row. Role Separator.

### `tooltip`

```odin
tooltip :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), anchor: Node, tip: string, loc := #caller_location)
```

tooltip attaches a short description to `anchor`: shown after the pointer rests on it for 600 ms, or while it has visible keyboard focus; hidden on leave, press or Escape. The text also becomes the anchor's accessible description, so assistive technology gets it without hovering.

### `text_trimmed`

```odin
text_trimmed :: proc(t: ^Tree, n: Node) -> bool
```

text_trimmed: the node is a text whose last layout cut it with "…", so the pointer resting on it gets the whole text as a tooltip (text builds that itself; nothing to register).

## image.odin

Image is an app-owned raster picture (RGBA8, sRGB) decoded once and drawn by `image` nodes or inside buttons. It is uploaded the first time it is drawn at any size and stays valid until image_destroy: into the picture atlas, or, when it is bigger than an atlas page (1022 px an edge) or drawn at under half its size, into a texture of its own with mip levels, up to the device's limit (16384 px on most). Change the pixels and bump `version` to have it re-uploaded. Pictures come and go freely: the renderer knows each by an identity given on its first draw (not its address, so a new picture where an old one was freed is never mistaken for it), and the pictures not drawn lately make way for new ones.

### `Image_Fit`

```odin
Image_Fit :: enum u8 { Cover, Stretch, Contain }
```

Image_Fit says what happens when the box's aspect differs from the picture's (or its `source`'s): Cover crops the picture centrally to fill the box (avatars, thumbnails); Stretch distorts it to fit; Contain shows all of it, letterboxed.

### `Image`

```odin
Image :: struct {
	width, height: int,
	pixels:        []u8, // RGBA8, row-major, owned
	// An animation: `frames` laid out in a grid of `cols` columns inside
	// `pixels`, so a frame is a source rectangle of one texture uploaded
	// once. 1 (or 0) frames is a still picture. A grid, not a column,
	// because a column overflows the GPU's texture limit (70 frames of
	// 480x270 stacked are 18,900 px tall; as a grid, 3,360 x 2,700).
	frames:        int,
	cols:          int,   // frames across; rows is ceil(frames / cols)
	delays:        []u16, // ms per frame, len == frames; owned with the pixels
	// The pixels are a secret: a QR code of a key, a picture decrypted in
	// memory. `image_destroy` overwrites them before freeing.
	secret:        bool,
	version:       u64,
	allocator:     runtime.Allocator,
	id:            u64,  // the atlas's name for it, given on its first draw (0 = not drawn yet); image_destroy clears it
	dirty:         [4]int, // image_touch: x0, y0, x1, y1 changed since it was last drawn (empty: x1 <= x0)
	touched:       u64,  // image_touch: how many times; a touched picture is drawn from a texture of its own
}
```

### `image_touch`

```odin
image_touch :: proc(img: ^Image, x, y, w, h: int)
```

image_touch says pixels `x, y, w, h` of `img` changed, where bumping `version` says all of them did: only that part is uploaded again and only the mip levels over it rebuilt, so a brush dab on a 4K canvas costs its few thousand pixels, not the whole picture. Touches add up until the picture is next drawn; an image node showing it paints again when the view is next built. A touched picture is drawn from a texture of its own from then on.

### `image_load`

```odin
image_load :: proc(data: []u8, allocator := context.allocator, max_pixels := IMAGE_LOAD_MAX_PIXELS) -> (img: Image, ok: bool)
```

image_load decodes a PNG, JPEG, GIF, WebP, BMP, ICO, QOI, TGA, netpbm or SVG file's bytes, telling which from the bytes themselves, into an Image in `allocator` (the caller's: image_destroy it). Any channel count and 8- or 16-bit depth are converted to RGBA8; a JPEG is turned the way its camera recorded it should be shown (EXIF orientation); an animated GIF or WebP becomes an animation the toolkit plays; an SVG is drawn at its own size. Unknown or malformed bytes give false, and so does a picture whose header declares more than `max_pixels`, refused before anything is allocated for it: a few kilobytes can claim gigabytes, which is how a received picture takes an app down. Odin's core decoders do the work: pure Odin, no C.

### `IMAGE_LOAD_MAX_PIXELS`

```odin
IMAGE_LOAD_MAX_PIXELS :: 128 * 1024 * 1024
```

IMAGE_LOAD_MAX_PIXELS is how many pixels image_load and a pasted picture accept by default: 128 million, a 100-megapixel camera's photo and some room, 512 MB once decoded. Pass `max_pixels` to image_load for more, or for less where pictures come from strangers.

### `image_from_rgba`

```odin
image_from_rgba :: proc(w, h: int, pixels: []u8, allocator := context.allocator, secret := false) -> (img: Image, ok: bool)
```

image_from_rgba wraps a copy of `pixels` (RGBA8, w × h).

### `image_destroy`

```odin
image_destroy :: proc(img: ^Image)
```

image_destroy frees a picture. A `secret` one has its pixels overwritten first, so the bytes are not left in freed memory.

### `image_secret`

```odin
image_secret :: proc(img: ^Image, on := true)
```

image_secret marks a picture's pixels a secret: a QR code of a key, an attachment decrypted in memory. `image_destroy` then overwrites them before freeing, as `secret_scrub` does for a keychain value. It covers the CPU copy only: once drawn, the renderer's copy lives in GPU memory the toolkit cannot overwrite, so treat being on screen as disclosure.

### `IMAGE_GRID_MAX_SIDE`

```odin
IMAGE_GRID_MAX_SIDE :: 4096
```

IMAGE_GRID_MAX_SIDE is how big a side an animation's texture may have when the caller names no limit: 4,096, which **every** conformant Vulkan device allows (it is the floor the specification promises). Most desktop cards allow 16,384, so an app that knows its device — `gpu_limits(g) .max_side` — can say so and keep more frames.

### `image_from_rgba_frames`

```odin
image_from_rgba_frames :: proc(w, h: int, frames: [][]u8, delays_ms: []int, max_side := IMAGE_GRID_MAX_SIDE, allocator := context.allocator) -> (img: Image, ok: bool)
```

image_from_rgba_frames makes an animation from `frames` pictures of `w` x `h` RGBA8 and a delay in milliseconds for each, which the app decoded itself (image_load returns GIF and WebP animations this way already). The toolkit animates it while it is on screen, with no message and no view rebuilt per frame, and shows the first frame under reduce-motion. The frames are copied into one texture as a square-ish grid, so they may be freed afterwards; the Image is the caller's, freed with `image_destroy`.  `max_side` caps each side of that texture (default `IMAGE_GRID_MAX_SIDE`: 120 frames of 480x270). If the frames cannot all fit, every k-th one is kept and the dropped ones' delays go to the one before, so the animation still runs to time: it loses smoothness, not sharpness. Compare `img.frames` with `len(frames)` to tell. A delay of 0 (older GIFs' "as fast as possible") is read as 100 ms, as every decoder does, and anything under 20 ms is raised to 20, so a GIF cannot spin the frame clock.

### `image_frame_size`

```odin
image_frame_size :: proc(img: ^Image) -> [2]int
```

image_frame_size is one frame's size in pixels — what an animation is to be laid out at, since `width` and `height` are the whole grid (so `height / frames` is wrong). A still picture answers its own size.

### `image_frame_rows`

```odin
image_frame_rows :: proc(img: ^Image) -> int
```

image_frame_rows is how many rows of frames the grid has, for an app drawing one frame itself with `source`.

### `image_frame_rect`

```odin
image_frame_rect :: proc(img: ^Image, i: int) -> Rect
```

image_frame_rect is the source rectangle of frame `i` in the grid — for an app that wants one frame of an animation rather than the animation: a still poster for a GIF that only plays when the pointer is on it, or a thumbnail. Pass it as `image`'s `source`, which also stops the toolkit animating it.

### `image`

```odin
image :: proc(ui: ^Ui($Msg), img: ^Image, width: f32 = 0, height: f32 = 0, radius: f32 = 0, label: string = "",
	fit: Image_Fit = .Cover, source: Rect = {}, pixelated: Maybe(bool) = nil, pixel_grid := false, key: u64 = 0, loc := #caller_location) -> Node
```

image draws `img` scaled into `width` × `height` (give one to keep the aspect ratio; neither → the pixel size), with `radius` corners. Decorative unless `label` names it (then role Image). `source` is the part of the picture shown, in its own pixels ({}: all of it): clamped to the picture, it is what `fit` and the missing side follow, and changing it every frame (a viewer's pan or zoom) is a repaint, never another upload. Drawn smaller than it is, it is sampled through mip levels.
